# grizzle findings

Defects and limits found while building `packages/pantry` on grizzle `1.0.0-beta.0` (`65203c4`), and re-checked at grizzle trunk `a1d5af1` (`a1d5af193cee117ab481f5897edd41101ff5d679`). The vendored commit is now trunk `1bf3dbb` (`1bf3dbb0a625949c086c28785f60b275d758b2b1`), `main`'s HEAD when it was pinned (see "The final pin" below); `dec8ab3`, `27c4cd2`, `4b155e8`, `fce1088`, `a2ff5eb` and `f66c05b` were the six pins before it, `64f0427` and `99b6217` the two before those, and `4016b69`, `48256f8` and `ee3b5fc` the three before them. Each finding is triaged against grizzle's source at that commit and classified as a limit or a defect (our own misuse is fixed here and not recorded); a defect carries its cause as `file:line` anchors and a reproduction that needs nothing of this repository, and a workaround is recorded beside the cause, never as a close. From 2026-11-06, by the operator's standing ruling relayed by grizzle-c5, qti-pie carries no workaround for any grizzle defect: it reports the defect and stays pinned where the code works, and the workarounds recorded below are history or are being removed.

| `grizzle migrate` applies every folder, then refuses at stage drift with `SQLSTATE 42804: collations are not supported by type text` (`DriftUnchecked`) when a relation is named `Text` and a later table has a text column; `grizzle drift` repeats it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | defect      | filed [bjornpagen/grizzle#4](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/issues/4); fixed at trunk `a1d5af1`. Every builtin type and function is printed schema-qualified or as a keyword (`packages/engine/src/sql/print.ts:84-111`), and the drift transaction reads under the closed path `pg_catalog, pg_temp` (`packages/grizzle/src/adapter/admission/drift.ts:76-85`; `packages/engine/src/sql/ast.ts:146`); tests `test/pg/migrate/catalog-names.test.ts:36-56`, `:208` and `test/pg/adapter/login-ambient.exclusive.test.ts`. Newest `main` (`6c2019d`) prints the same way (research a39e6a25, by source reading); checked at a1d5af1 (re-checked de85a11c) and 6c2019d                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | The drift check replays the snapshot under `SET LOCAL search_path TO <scratch>, pg_catalog, pg_temp` (`packages/grizzle/src/adapter/admission/drift.ts:505` to 507; the replay at `508` to `510`; the path reset for the reads at `511`), so the scratch schema is searched before `pg_catalog`. `printType` writes a builtin type bare (`packages/engine/src/sql/print.ts:174` to 175), on the premise of the `builtinTypeNames` comment that such names "resolve ahead of any user schema" (`packages/engine/src/sql/ast.ts:32` to 35). The text domains are declared `AS text COLLATE pg_catalog.pg_c_utf8` (`packages/engine/src/physical/ddl/domains.ts:113`, `packages/engine/src/text/refinement.ts:424`, doc comments for the DDL `printType` writes). Once the replay creates the table `text`, a later bare `text` names that table's composite row type, which takes no collation. The error surfaces as `DriftUnchecked` (`packages/grizzle/src/adapter/migrate/errors.ts:629`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `relation("Text", { id: uuid, value: str.regex("^.*$", "su") }, ["id"])` and `relation("Tree", { id: uuid, label: str.regex("^.*$", "su") }, ["id"])` in one schema with `logins: []`; `grizzle generate --name init`, then `grizzle migrate` into an empty database on PostgreSQL 19rc1. `Text` alone migrates clean (a table's own columns resolve before its row type exists). Confirmed outside this repository in a scratch package of the two vendored tarballs: `grizzle migrate` into an empty database exits 1 with `grizzle migrate: refused at stage drift (schema repro_drift, SQLSTATE 42804): the catalog of repro_drift could not be read: SQLSTATE 42804: collations are not supported by type text`, and `grizzle drift` repeats it. With `Text` renamed `TextNode`, migrate and drift pass. | A relation named like a builtin type the printer writes bare, followed by a table with a column of that type and no COLLATE, gives a false `Drifted` instead of an error: the scratch replay binds the bare type to the shadowing composite while the installed table holds the real type. Write case, measured on a lease database in this repository: one ChangeSet with a member for every relation `Qti` writes (630 relations, 216 `Node` arms) is refused at `db.apply` with `ResourceBound at write program ... budgets.sqlBytes <= 1048576 (needed 1608527)` on content (13 migrations); packaging's whole roster needs 1,056,648 (9 migrations). A refusal reports only the charge that crossed the ceiling: 200 relations compile at 768,768 bytes, 400 are refused at 1,050,796, all 630 at 1,085,990 (`p2-53`, `4e33e71`), and the whole-program 1,608,527 came from the database-free `Db.open` probe of `p2-52` (`dd0be7c`). One relation costs 2.5 to 6.3 KB of SQL, so 800 KiB holds about 130 to 320. Real packages write at most 90 of about 630 relations today, and 83 of 91 content fixtures are admitted (head-cto ruling `1ce3cfbc`; the byte sizes of those fixtures are unmeasured). The refusal is raised before any SQL, is not retried, and carries grizzle's raw error. Read case on M1: the export's 23 programs all compile, the largest (`nodeQuery7`) at 1,997 nodes, 645 fields and 197,334 SQL bytes, about 20 percent of the ceilings (`p2-53`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | The read split into runs of 30 attachments, each its own statement (`src/export/queries.ts`; `p1-65`, `9dc3fd7`). Cost: the runs are separate statements, so a tree written during a read could be read in two states. Considered and declined (mail `3609cdb5` item 1, decision 70): reading the tree in one ChangeSet of `.read` steps, for one compilation and one snapshot. A content export program costs about 67 nodes and 6.6 KB per attachment (30 attachments compiled at 1,997 nodes, 645 fields and 197,334 SQL bytes), so one compilation holds about 150 attachments by nodes and 159 by `sqlBytes`; this tree has 845 attachments in 29 runs (a database-free load of `nodeRuns`; the spec estimated about 735), so a whole-tree read needs at least six ChangeSets and no single snapshot. The runs read one tree whole because the store is append-only (`src/export/queries.ts`, the comment over `nodeQueries`). Write case: none built. The import stays one ChangeSet in one transaction (decision 69); a split write loses atomicity, since no transaction spans ChangeSets (`README.md:1444`, `:1694`, `:1892`) and laws are checked at each commit (`README.md:1252`), so a split can only group `Node` rows under a marker protocol that is not built. Phase 13 gates on the largest admitted package's compiled program at or under 819,200 bytes and maps a refusal to a named import refusal. The export keeps its runs of 30, not for a budget but for build time (A1 F3: one 832-attachment query takes 11 to 15 s to build against 1.5 to 1.7 s for the 28 runs)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `p1-65` (`9dc3fd7`), `p2-52` (`dd0be7c`), `p2-53` (`4e33e71`)                                                                                                                                            |

| No quotation punctuation immediately before, after or inside a `q` cannot be stated as a grizzle statement: rule 15 reads the last character of the sibling one position before the `q`, the first character of the one after it, and the first and last character of its own content; the browser draws the quotes, so a typed one doubles them. The limit is adjacency across sibling rows by position. The character set is our choice, not grizzle's and not the spec's (spec `:1318` names only `"`): the double quote, the single and low quotes `‘` `‚` `‛`, `“` `”` `„` `‟`, and the guillemets `«` `»` `‹` `›`, with `&quot;` read as `"`. `extend` at `:149`). `StatementsWithin` (`packages/engine/src/types/schema/schema.ts:104-108`) takes `NamedBy` of every statement (`:66-83`, over `Statements[number]`) and looks each named relation up in the schema's index with `Undeclared` (`:90-98`), so the instantiation count grows with the statements and the relations they name, and `tsc` refuses past its own instantiation limit. The opt-out is documented in the source, in the doc comment of `Undeclared` (`:85-89`): "a statement list typed ReadonlyArray<Statement> is not refused here; the run time checks it", and `schema()` still refuses every unknown relation at build ("names B, which no level of the schema declares"). The README names neither the ceiling nor the opt-out. A limit, not a defect: the check is a deliberate type-level fold with a documented skip. Not isolated: whether the statement count or the count of relations they name drives the depth, and the exact depth `tsc` counts. Traced at `93ff55b`: TS2589 here is TypeScript's per-statement instantiation count (`instantiationCount >= 5_000_000`), not the depth limit, and the count grew as about 10.6·N² (N 100: 148,105; 200: 490,805; 400: 1,776,205; 600: 3,861,605; 698: 5,175,491; 699: TS2589). `Undeclared` looked each named relation up through `IndexOfRelations`, `{ [K in keyof Rs as NameOf<Rs[K]>]: Rs[K] }` (`types/schema/schema.ts:84` at `394d2d8`), with `Lookup`'s `N extends keyof Index`, and the keys of a remapped mapped type are recomputed at every `keyof`. ### Feature requests considered

Rows taken through `feature-request-authoring` on 2026-11-02, none filed (rows by table position). Row 5, PostgreSQL roles named from the schema's declared name: reproduced (the second migrate refuses, SQLSTATE 42710, role "blog__web" already exists); not filed, because role names are literal in the law-function bodies (grizzle `laws/residual/sql.ts:163-173`), policy names and role schemas, and drift and admission match them by name against `snapshot.json`, so an opt-in namespace is a redesign; the operator chose not to file. Row 16, the RFC 3986 grammar against the fixed regex budget: not filed; `policy.ts:4-7` rules out configurable thresholds and the bounds are measured (`text/refinement.ts:42-54`); our evidence (strict RFC 3986 at 1,982 constructs compiles in 2.3 ms on 19rc1) is one pattern; the operator chose not to file. Rows 20 and 29, pattern refusals that name no column: not a request; a named contract per column makes the refusal name it (`{"kind":"contract","contract":"UserEmail"}`; grizzle `domains.ts:49-52` and `:112`), run on the cluster; columns linked by containment must share one contract, so that pair stays unnamed; operator ruling `692aa1e6` (2) adopts a contract per concept (phase 13). Row 21, the reason classes type only: intended, `README.md:1622` "Reasons are types only"; not a request. Row 23, the compile budget: not a request; `policy.ts:4-7` says none of grizzle's thresholds is configurable, and several `.read` steps in one ChangeSet run in one transaction (`README.md:1456`, `:1746`), which removes the consistency cost of splitting a read (true for reads only: a split write loses atomicity, since no transaction spans ChangeSets, `README.md:1444`, `:1694`, `:1892`); compiled without a database: 6 reads of 28 attachments (168) in one ChangeSet, and about 180 to 210 is the shared cap. The write case of the same row (one `db.apply` against the same 1 MiB of SQL; the README's Limits section names no compile budget) has not been taken through `feature-request-authoring`; it goes there only on the operator's yes.

### Drift replay puts the scratch schema before pg_catalog, so relations named Text, Uuid or Money shadow builtin types

````text
### Summary
The drift replay runs with the scratch schema ahead of `pg_catalog` while builtin types print without a schema, so a relation named like a builtin type shadows that type in every later statement of the replay. A relation named `Text` gives SQLSTATE 42804 (`collations are not supported by type text`) from `grizzle migrate` and `grizzle drift`, at stage `drift`, after every migration folder has applied. A relation named `Uuid` or `Money` gives a false `Drifted` of two facts instead of an error. The shadowed statements come after the shadowing relation in grizzle's table order in the migration, not in declaration order: `{ Tree, Text }` fails the same way, with the same snapshot `b506347d66f2a33f`.

Ran with `@bjornpagen/grizzle` 1.0.0-beta.0 packed from `65203c4`, Node v26.13.0, against an empty database on PostgreSQL 19rc1, which is grizzle's own development cluster (built by `pnpm pg:build`).

### Reproduction
1. In `grizzle.config.ts` of a package that depends on `@bjornpagen/grizzle`, declare a schema with two relations and no logins.

```ts
import { defineConfig, relation, schema, str, uuid } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"

const Prose = str.regex("^.*$", "su")
const Text = relation("Text", { id: uuid, value: Prose }, ["id"])
const Tree = relation("Tree", { id: uuid, label: Prose }, ["id"])
const ReproDrift = schema("ReproDrift", { Text, Tree }, [])

export default defineConfig({ logins: [], schemas: [ReproDrift] })
```

Every text field prints as `text COLLATE "pg_catalog"."pg_c_utf8"` (the README says text always carries a pattern), and `^.*$` makes no domain, so `label` is a plain text column with a collation.

2. Generate the first migration.

```console
$ pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init
```

3. Migrate into an empty database, with `DATABASE_URL` naming the owner role and that database.

```console
$ pnpm exec grizzle migrate
grizzle migrate: refused at stage drift (schema repro_drift, SQLSTATE 42804): the catalog of repro_drift could not be read: SQLSTATE 42804: collations are not supported by type text
Every folder applied, but the catalog differs from the snapshot.
fix: undo the change made outside a migration, or reach the snapshot with a new migration (grizzle generate --custom)
$ echo $?
1
```

4. `grizzle drift` against the same database exits 1 with the same first line and ends with `Nothing was applied; the catalog differs from the snapshot.`

**Control.** The same two relations with `Text` renamed `TextNode` apply and pass drift.

```console
$ pnpm exec grizzle migrate
ReproDrift: applied 1 migration, now at 9c3dcc88e9e5edea
  20261101204352_init: 01-setup.sql, 02-structure.sql, 03-publish.sql
$ echo $?
0
```

`grizzle drift` then exits 0.

### Same statements in psql
Two `CREATE TABLE` statements under the search path the replay uses reproduce the 42804 without grizzle. The session ran as one transaction on the same PostgreSQL 19rc1 cluster, with `psql -a`, and was rolled back. Part a is the `Text` case, with the same statements under `pg_catalog, s, pg_temp` as the control. Part b is the `Uuid` case, followed by a catalog read that does not depend on the search path. The search path order is [the replay's](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/src/adapter/admission/drift.ts#L505-L507).

```text
ERROR:  collations are not supported by type text
LINE 1: ...EATE TABLE s."tree" (id uuid NOT NULL, label text COLLATE "p...

 format_type
-------------
 uuid

 attname | typnamespace | typname | typtype
---------+--------------+---------+---------
 id      | s            | uuid    | c
```

`format_type` omits the schema because `s` is first on the path, and the catalog row shows that the type of `vote.id` is the composite `s.uuid`, not `pg_catalog.uuid`. Under `pg_catalog, s, pg_temp` both `CREATE TABLE` statements of part a succeed.

<details>
<summary>The whole psql session, verbatim</summary>

```text
BEGIN;
BEGIN
CREATE SCHEMA s;
CREATE SCHEMA
-- Part a: grizzle's order puts the scratch schema first.
SAVEPOINT a;
SAVEPOINT
SET LOCAL search_path TO s, pg_catalog, pg_temp;
SET
CREATE TABLE s."text" (id uuid NOT NULL, value text COLLATE "pg_catalog"."pg_c_utf8" NOT NULL);
CREATE TABLE
CREATE TABLE s."tree" (id uuid NOT NULL, label text COLLATE "pg_catalog"."pg_c_utf8" NOT NULL);
ERROR:  collations are not supported by type text
LINE 1: ...EATE TABLE s."tree" (id uuid NOT NULL, label text COLLATE "p...
                                                             ^
ROLLBACK TO a;
ROLLBACK
-- Part a, control: pg_catalog first.
SAVEPOINT b;
SAVEPOINT
SET LOCAL search_path TO pg_catalog, s, pg_temp;
SET
CREATE TABLE s."text" (id uuid NOT NULL, value text COLLATE "pg_catalog"."pg_c_utf8" NOT NULL);
CREATE TABLE
CREATE TABLE s."tree" (id uuid NOT NULL, label text COLLATE "pg_catalog"."pg_c_utf8" NOT NULL);
CREATE TABLE
ROLLBACK TO b;
ROLLBACK
-- Part b: a table named uuid, then a column declared uuid.
SAVEPOINT c;
SAVEPOINT
SET LOCAL search_path TO s, pg_catalog, pg_temp;
SET
CREATE TABLE s."uuid" (id uuid NOT NULL);
CREATE TABLE
CREATE TABLE s."vote" (id uuid NOT NULL);
CREATE TABLE
SELECT format_type(atttypid, atttypmod) FROM pg_attribute WHERE attrelid = 's.vote'::regclass AND attname = 'id';
 format_type
-------------
 uuid
(1 row)

SELECT a.attname, t.typnamespace::regnamespace, t.typname, t.typtype FROM pg_attribute a JOIN pg_type t ON t.oid = a.atttypid WHERE a.attrelid = 's.vote'::regclass AND a.attname = 'id';
 attname | typnamespace | typname | typtype
---------+--------------+---------+---------
 id      | s            | uuid    | c
(1 row)

ROLLBACK TO c;
ROLLBACK
ROLLBACK;
ROLLBACK
```

</details>

After the refused `grizzle migrate`, `pg_attribute` joined to `pg_type` shows `tree.label` as `pg_catalog.text` with collation `pg_c_utf8` and `vote.id` as `pg_catalog.uuid`, and the installing session's `search_path` is `"$user", public`. The installed catalog matches the snapshot and only the replay differs. In the `Uuid` and `Vote` run below, the replay produced `vote.id` as `repro_drift.uuid`, which the report calls missing from the database, while the database has `pg_catalog.uuid`, which the report calls unexpected.

### Other names
**Uuid.** Relations `Uuid` and `Vote`, declared like step 1 as `relation("Uuid", { id: uuid }, ["id"])` and `relation("Vote", { id: uuid }, ["id"])` in schema `{ Uuid, Vote }`, give a drift refusal that names two facts. No error is raised for the type.

```console
$ pnpm exec grizzle migrate
grizzle migrate: refused at stage drift (schema repro_drift): the catalog of repro_drift differs from snapshot 4f582aeba74b8a2f20a5389725da2c79087b76eb7ebf8df6fd70855df6fb3f16 by 2 facts (edits are allowed, drift is not)
  missing column repro_drift.vote.id as repro_drift.uuid; not null
  unexpected column repro_drift.vote.id as uuid; not null
Every folder applied, but the catalog differs from the snapshot.
fix: undo the change made outside a migration, or reach the snapshot with a new migration (grizzle generate --custom)
$ echo $?
1
```

**Money.** Relations `relation("Money", { id: uuid }, ["id"])` and `relation("Wallet", { amount: pg.money, id: uuid }, ["id"])` give the same two facts for `wallet.amount`, with `grizzle migrate` exiting 1. The run declared two more relations, `Int4range` and `Slot` with `span(pg.integer)`, and `Money` and `Wallet` alone were not run. `grizzle drift` was not run on this schema.

```console
$ pnpm exec grizzle migrate
grizzle migrate: refused at stage drift (schema repro_drift): the catalog of repro_drift differs from snapshot fd253792c387d2daa5535237c40e54fadfe68be014df5046511cd421efb1bd1e by 2 facts (edits are allowed, drift is not)
  unexpected column repro_drift.wallet.amount as money; not null
  missing column repro_drift.wallet.amount as repro_drift.money; not null
Every folder applied, but the catalog differs from the snapshot.
fix: undo the change made outside a migration, or reach the snapshot with a new migration (grizzle generate --custom)
```

### Expected
The folders that applied install the snapshot's catalog, so [`grizzle drift`](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/README.md#L1528-L1529) ("does the database match the snapshot?") reports a match and `grizzle migrate` exits 0, for each schema above. The README, the packaged agent guide (`packages/grizzle/skills/grizzle/SKILL.md`) and the source were searched for a rule that reserves or refuses a relation name equal to a builtin type, and none was found. `grizzle generate` and `grizzle check` print ok for each schema above.

### What changes the result
  | Relations | `grizzle migrate` | `grizzle drift` |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | `Text`, `Tree` | refused, SQLSTATE 42804 | refused, SQLSTATE 42804 |
  | `Text` | applied, exit 0 | matches |
  | `TextNode`, `Tree` | applied, exit 0 | matches |
  | `Tree`, `Text`, declared in that order | refused, SQLSTATE 42804, snapshot `b506347d66f2a33f` | refused, SQLSTATE 42804 |
  | `Uuid`, `Vote` | refused, 2 facts | refused, the same 2 facts |
  | `Int4range`, `Money`, `Slot`, `Wallet` | refused, 2 facts, both on `wallet.amount` | not run |

### Cause
Working theory. The replay binds a bare builtin type name through `search_path`, and a relation of the same name shadows it.

1. `driftFlight` replays the snapshot under `SET LOCAL search_path TO <scratch schema>, pg_catalog, pg_temp`, so the scratch schema is searched before `pg_catalog` ([drift.ts](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/src/adapter/admission/drift.ts#L505-L507)). PostgreSQL searches `pg_catalog` first only when the path does not list it ([The System Catalog Schema](https://www.postgresql.org/docs/current/ddl-schemas.html#DDL-SCHEMAS-CATALOG)).
2. `printType` writes a builtin type without a schema ([print.ts](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/sql/print.ts#L174-L175)).
3. The comment on `builtinTypeNames` says these names "resolve ahead of any user schema, so they print unquoted" ([ast.ts](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/sql/ast.ts#L32-L35)). Under the replay's path, `text`, `uuid` and `money` resolve to the scratch schema first, as the psql session shows.
4. Once the replay creates the table `text`, its row type is what a bare `text` names in every later statement. A composite type takes no collation, which gives SQLSTATE 42804 for a column declared with one. A later column declared without a collation binds to the composite without an error, which gives the false `Drifted`.
5. The refusal surfaces as `DriftUnchecked` ([errors.ts](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/src/adapter/migrate/errors.ts#L629-L666)).

### Impact
A schema with such a relation cannot be installed through grizzle at all, because `grizzle migrate` exits 1 on every run even though every folder applied. The printed fix line, "undo the change made outside a migration", points at a change that was never made.

### Not confirmed
- Whether these names are meant to be refused at compile time. No rule was found in the README, the packaged agent guide or the compiler, and `grizzle generate` and `grizzle check` accept each schema above.
- Relations named `Regclass`, `Record` or `Trigger` were not run. The range names are not reachable through a relation name: the name converter writes `Int4range` as `int_4_range`. `jsonb` has no field type. A closed enum or a contract named `Text`, `Uuid` or `Money` was not run.
- Not run on an unpatched PostgreSQL.

### Fix direction
Possible change, untested. Either run the replay under `SET LOCAL search_path TO pg_catalog, <scratch schema>, pg_temp`, or run it under `pg_catalog, pg_temp` with no scratch schema, as [drift.ts](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/src/adapter/admission/drift.ts#L511) already does for the reads that follow. Every object the replay creates carries its schema (`ObjectName.schema` is required in [ast.ts](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/sql/ast.ts#L5-L8), and the [scratch hooks](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/src/adapter/admission/drift.ts#L275-L286) move named types, functions and extension objects into the scratch schema), so neither option changes what the replay creates.

### Acceptance check
For each of the `Text` and `Tree`, `Uuid` and `Vote`, and `Money` and `Wallet` schemas, `grizzle migrate` into an empty database exits 0 and `grizzle drift` afterwards reports a match.

---
**AI disclosure:** Written by AI, checked by independent AI reviewers, and filed without the account holder's review.
- **Provider:** Anthropic
- **Model:** Sonnet 5.5
````

### Draft: the alternatives law function with an unarmed handle (row 27)

Title: `alternatives law function: first call per backend grows as the square of tables x handles, about 8 times more with an unarmed handle`

````text
### Summary
The first call of the `alternatives` reverse law function in each backend compiles a body with one `WHEN` per (participating table x handle) cell, plus one `IF NOT FOUND` refusal per cell when a handle has no arm, and that compile time grows as the square of the cell count. In `grizzle generate` output with one unarmed handle the first call takes 0.36 s at 43 handles, 5.25 s at 85 and 25.72 s at 128, and with every handle armed it takes 0.62 s at 85 and 2.99 s at 128. The unarmed handle multiplies the cost about 8 times at the same size (59,008 lines against 15,578 at 85 handles).

Ran `grizzle generate` on a standalone schema and timed the generated functions on PostgreSQL 19rc1 (grizzle's development cluster, `jit = off`), one run per row. Read at `bjornpagen/grizzle@65203c4`.

### Code path
1. The doc comment of `alternativesFamily` states that an omitted handle makes the `CASE` need an `ELSE`, and that "with an ELSE each armed branch raises after its own probe; with every handle armed the one refusal follows END CASE".

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/families/alternatives/index.ts#L34-L36

2. `armed` is true only when every handle has an arm.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/families/alternatives/index.ts#L44

3. When `armed` is false each `WHEN` body is the probe plus `unlessFound(parent.raise)`, so the refusal block is emitted once per arm.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/families/alternatives/index.ts#L57

4. When `armed` is true the refusal is emitted once, after the `CASE`. When it is false the dispatch carries `else: []` and no trailing refusal.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/families/alternatives/index.ts#L61-L64

5. The residual skeleton dispatches on `TG_TABLE_NAME` over every participating table and splices the family's steps into each branch, so the whole `CASE` is repeated once per branch, armed or not.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/residual/skeleton.ts#L205-L210

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/residual/skeleton.ts#L315-L316

### Reproduction
1. Create a package that depends on `@bjornpagen/grizzle` 1.0.0-beta.0 and `effect` 4.0.7 (the versions these runs used), installed as [the README installs them](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/README.md#L853-L854) (`pnpm add @bjornpagen/grizzle effect`, then `pnpm exec grizzle ...`), on Node 26.13.0. Add the two files below.

2. Save `schema.ts`, a closed roster of three handles with an arm and one without, and `grizzle.config.ts`.

```ts
import { alternatives, closed, closedId, login, relation, role, schema, uuid } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"

export const Kind = closed("Kind", ["k1", "k2", "k3", "unarmed"])
export const Node = relation("Node", { id: uuid, kind: closedId(Kind) }, ["id"])
export const A1 = relation("A1", { id: uuid }, ["id"])
export const A2 = relation("A2", { id: uuid }, ["id"])
export const A3 = relation("A3", { id: uuid }, ["id"])
export const Repro = schema("Repro", { A1, A2, A3, Kind, Node }, [alternatives(Node, "kind", Kind, { k1: A1, k2: A2, k3: A3 })])
const stored = { A1, A2, A3, Node }
export const Author = role(Repro, "author", stored, { write: ["A1", "A2", "A3", "Node"] })
export const App = login(Repro, "app", [Author], { workload: [] })
```

```ts
import { defineConfig } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"
import { App, Repro } from "./schema.ts"
export default defineConfig({ logins: [App], schemas: [Repro] })
```

<details>
<summary>schema.ts for the 43-, 85- and 128-handle runs (parametrized; at ARMS=3 UNARMED=1 its migration SQL is identical to the schema above)</summary>

`ARMS` sets the number of handles with an arm, and `UNARMED=1` adds one handle to the roster that has no arm. The casts widen the literal key types that `Object.fromEntries` loses. At `ARMS=3 UNARMED=1` the `.sql` files of its migration folder and of the literal schema's folder have no `diff`.

```ts
import { alternatives, closed, closedId, login, relation, role, schema, uuid } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"

const arms = Number(process.env.ARMS ?? "3")
const unarmed = process.env.UNARMED === "1"
const handles = Array.from({ length: arms }, (_, i) => `k${i + 1}`)
export const Kind = closed("Kind", [...handles, ...(unarmed ? ["unarmed"] : [])] as [string, ...string[]])
export const Node = relation("Node", { id: uuid, kind: closedId(Kind) }, ["id"])
const armRelations = Object.fromEntries(handles.map((h, i) => [`A${i + 1}`, relation(`A${i + 1}`, { id: uuid }, ["id"])]))
const armMap = Object.fromEntries(handles.map((h, i) => [h, armRelations[`A${i + 1}`]]))
export const Repro = schema("Repro", { Kind, Node, ...armRelations } as any, [alternatives(Node, "kind", Kind, armMap as any)] as any)
const stored = { Node, ...armRelations } as any
export const Author = role(Repro, "author", stored, { write: Object.keys(stored) } as any)
export const App = login(Repro, "app", [Author], { workload: [] })
```

</details>

3. Generate each size with one unarmed handle and with every handle armed, each run in an empty `migrations` folder (moved away before the next run), and read the function in `03-validate.sql` of each migration folder.

```sh
ARMS=85 UNARMED=1 pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init
ARMS=85 UNARMED=0 pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init
```

The same two commands ran with `ARMS` 43 and 128. At `ARMS=128 UNARMED=1` the command exits with `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` in `printPlBlock`, and so does `ARMS=123 UNARMED=1`; `ARMS=120 UNARMED=1` and `ARMS=128 UNARMED=0` run. The 128-handle folder with one unarmed handle came from running the CLI's entry file with `node --stack-size=1500`.

```text
UNARMED=1: function 59,008 lines, 5,464,132 bytes; WHEN 7,310, RAISE 7,310, TG_TABLE_NAME 85, ELSE 86; snapshot.json 99,437,981 bytes
UNARMED=0: function 15,578 lines,   917,054 bytes; WHEN 7,310, RAISE    86, TG_TABLE_NAME 85, ELSE  0; snapshot.json  17,633,763 bytes
```

The counts are the function's lines and bytes, and the occurrences of each keyword inside it. At `ARMS=3 UNARMED=1` the function has the shape the doc comment describes: the probe and `IF NOT FOUND THEN RAISE ... END IF;` in every `WHEN`, an `ELSE` with an empty body, no trailing refusal, and the whole `CASE` in each `TG_TABLE_NAME` branch.

4. Install each folder in an empty database, as the database's owner, with grizzle's own files. `01-setup.sql`, `02-structure.sql` and `03-validate.sql` are plain SQL and create no role, so `04-publish.sql` is not run. Each command is its own `psql` process (one process is one backend). `03-validate.sql` starts with `LOCK TABLE`, so it runs in one transaction, and its `CREATE FUNCTION` line is the compile.

```sh
psql -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f 01-setup.sql -f 02-structure.sql
psql -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -1 -c '\timing on' -f 03-validate.sql
psql -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c '\timing on' -f probe.sql
psql -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c '\timing on' -f probe2.sql
```

`probe.sql` and `probe2.sql` are two transactions each that insert a `Node` row of kind `k1` and its `A1` row (`repro.a_1`), so the deferred trigger fires at each `COMMIT`. `probe.sql` is the first backend and `probe2.sql` a second one.

```sh
mk() { printf "BEGIN;\nINSERT INTO repro.node VALUES ('00000000-0000-0000-0000-00000000000$1', 'k1');\nINSERT INTO repro.a_1 VALUES ('00000000-0000-0000-0000-00000000000$1');\nCOMMIT;\n"; }
{ mk 1; mk 2; } > probe.sql
{ mk 3; mk 4; } > probe2.sql
```

<details>
<summary>psql output of the two 85-handle folders, PostgreSQL 19rc1, `jit = off`. The label line is `wc -lc` of `03-validate.sql`; the rest is the `Time:` line after each `CREATE FUNCTION` and `COMMIT`.</summary>

```text
== ARMS=85 UNARMED=1, 03-validate.sql:    59456 5535413
CREATE FUNCTION
Time: 5166.239 ms (00:05.166)
COMMIT
Time: 5248.805 ms (00:05.249)
COMMIT
Time: 0.195 ms
COMMIT
Time: 5096.081 ms (00:05.096)
COMMIT
Time: 0.136 ms
== ARMS=85 UNARMED=0, 03-validate.sql:    16026  988335
CREATE FUNCTION
Time: 645.118 ms
COMMIT
Time: 624.333 ms
COMMIT
Time: 0.174 ms
COMMIT
Time: 624.982 ms
COMMIT
Time: 0.199 ms
```

</details>

The first `COMMIT` of each backend pays the cost and the second takes under 1 ms. The unarmed function takes 5.25 s against 0.62 s for the all-armed function (**Control.** the same schema with `UNARMED=0`).

5. Scaling tool. `gen-fix.sh` writes SQL in the shapes `grizzle generate` emits (`per-arm` with one unarmed handle, `trailing` with every handle armed), plus the `fixed` and `naive` shapes of Fix direction, so the 216-handle rows and the `fixed` rows of the table below can be varied without a generate run. Its `read` strips the leading spaces of the first line of the refusal block, which changes whitespace only.

<details>
<summary>gen-fix.sh (bash, 43 lines)</summary>

```sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# usage: gen-fix.sh <per-arm|trailing|fixed|naive> <arms> <branches>   prints one SQL file
set -euo pipefail
shape=$1 arms=$2 branches=$3
read -r -d '' raise_text <<'RAISE' || true
          IF NOT FOUND THEN
            RAISE EXCEPTION 'law __node_kind_alternatives violated'
              USING ERRCODE = 'integrity_constraint_violation'::text,
                    CONSTRAINT = '__node_kind_alternatives'::text,
                    DETAIL = CASE WHEN current_setting('is_superuser'::text) = 'on'::text OR CASE WHEN current_setting('role'::text) = 'none'::text THEN SESSION_USER::text ELSE current_setting('role'::text) END IN (CURRENT_USER::text, 'app__author'::text) THEN 'w["Node",'::text || to_jsonb("p"."c1"::text)::text || ']'::text ELSE 'w["Node"]'::text END;
          END IF;
RAISE
{
  echo 'CREATE SCHEMA repro_law;'
  printf "CREATE TYPE repro_law.kind AS ENUM ("
  for ((j = 1; j <= arms; j++)); do ((j > 1)) && printf ', '; printf "'k%d'" "$j"; done
  [[ $shape != trailing ]] && printf ", 'unarmed'"
  echo ');'
  echo 'CREATE TABLE repro_law.node (id uuid PRIMARY KEY, kind repro_law.kind NOT NULL);'
  for ((j = 1; j <= arms; j++)); do echo "CREATE TABLE repro_law.a$j (id uuid PRIMARY KEY);"; done
  echo 'CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION repro_law.law() RETURNS trigger LANGUAGE plpgsql VOLATILE SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = pg_catalog, pg_temp SET plan_cache_mode = force_custom_plan AS $$'
  echo 'DECLARE'; echo '  "p" record;'; echo 'BEGIN'
  for ((i = 0; i < branches; i++)); do
    if ((i == 0)); then echo "  IF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'node'::text THEN"; ref='NEW'
    else echo "  ELSIF TG_TABLE_NAME = 'a$(((i - 1) % arms + 1))'::text THEN"; ref='OLD'; fi
    echo "    FOR \"p\" IN SELECT \"r1\".\"id\" AS \"c1\", \"r1\".\"kind\" AS \"c2\" FROM repro_law.node AS \"r1\" WHERE \"r1\".\"id\" = $ref.\"id\" LOOP"
    echo '      CASE "p"."c2"'
    for ((j = 1; j <= arms; j++)); do
      echo "        WHEN 'k$j'::repro_law.kind THEN"
      echo "          PERFORM 1 FROM repro_law.a$j AS \"r1\" WHERE \"r1\".\"id\" = \"p\".\"c1\";"
      [[ $shape == per-arm ]] && echo "$raise_text"
    done
    [[ $shape != trailing ]] && echo "        ELSE"; [[ $shape == fixed ]] && echo "          PERFORM 1;"
    echo '      END CASE;'
    [[ $shape != per-arm ]] && echo "$raise_text"
    echo '    END LOOP;'
  done
  echo '  END IF;'
  echo "  IF TG_OP = 'DELETE'::text THEN RETURN OLD; END IF;"
  echo '  RETURN NEW;'
  echo 'END $$;'
  echo 'CREATE CONSTRAINT TRIGGER law_node AFTER INSERT ON repro_law.node DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION repro_law.law();'
}
```

</details>

The probe for its tables:

```sh
mk() { printf "BEGIN;\nINSERT INTO repro_law.node VALUES ('00000000-0000-0000-0000-00000000000$1', 'k1');\nINSERT INTO repro_law.a1 VALUES ('00000000-0000-0000-0000-00000000000$1');\nCOMMIT;\n"; }
{ mk 1; mk 2; } > probe.sql
{ mk 3; mk 4; } > probe2.sql
```

Between runs, `DROP SCHEMA repro_law CASCADE`. Each shape is one file run, then `probe.sql`, then `probe2.sql`, each as its own `psql` process with `\timing on`:

```sh
./gen-fix.sh trailing 216 215 > a.sql
psql -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c '\timing on' -f a.sql
psql -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c '\timing on' -f probe.sql
psql -X -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -c '\timing on' -f probe2.sql
```

### Expected
The README's flagship `alternatives` example leaves a handle unarmed: `open` has no arm in `alternatives(Invoice, "state", InvoiceState, { paid: Payment, void: Voiding })`, so the unarmed case is the documented main path, and the Limits list names no law-function size or first-call cost.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/README.md#L639

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/README.md#L1881

The sibling code sets the contract. The same function with every handle armed emits one refusal per branch, after `END CASE`.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/families/alternatives/index.ts#L64

The `fixed` shape under Fix direction returns the same results as the per-arm shape in five cases, at 1.1 percent more lines than the all-armed shape. The semantics the doc comment describes do not require the per-arm size, so a schema with an unarmed handle can generate a function about as large, and as fast on its first call, as the all-armed one.

### What changes the result
`grizzle generate` rows give the size of the function, and `gen-fix.sh` rows the size of the whole file, DDL included. `Handles` is the number of handles with an arm, and `Branches` the number of participating tables. Times round to 0.01 s, and the fold of step 4 holds psql's lines for the 85-handle rows.

  | Source | Shape | Handles / branches | Size | First `COMMIT` | Second `COMMIT` |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `grizzle generate` | one unarmed handle | 43 / 44 | 15,412 lines, 1.42 MB | 0.36 s | 0.2 ms |
  | `grizzle generate` | every handle armed | 43 / 44 | 4,280 lines, 0.25 MB | 0.05 s | 0.2 ms |
  | `grizzle generate` | one unarmed handle | 85 / 86 | 59,008 lines, 5.46 MB | 5.25 s | 0.2 ms |
  | `grizzle generate` | every handle armed | 85 / 86 | 15,578 lines, 0.92 MB | 0.62 s | 0.2 ms |
  | `grizzle generate` | one unarmed handle | 128 / 129 | 132,882 lines, 12.34 MB | 25.72 s | 0.2 ms |
  | `grizzle generate` | every handle armed | 128 / 129 | 34,455 lines, 2.03 MB | 2.99 s | 0.2 ms |
  | `gen-fix.sh` | `trailing`, every handle armed | 216 / 215 | 95,473 lines, 5.57 MB | 24.91 s | 0.3 ms |
  | `gen-fix.sh` | `fixed` (see Fix direction) | 85 / 84 | 15,469 lines, 0.90 MB | 0.62 s | 0.3 ms |
  | `gen-fix.sh` | `fixed` (see Fix direction) | 216 / 215 | 95,903 lines, 5.58 MB | 24.41 s | 0.2 ms |

The first call in each backend pays the cost, and a later call in the same backend does not. A second backend pays it again: 5.10 s with one unarmed handle and 0.62 s with every handle armed, at 85 handles.

**The first call grows as the square of the cell count.** With one unarmed handle the cells (handles x branches) are 1,892, 7,310 and 16,512, and the first `COMMIT` is 0.36 s, 5.25 s and 25.72 s: ratios of 14.4 and 4.9 against 14.9 and 5.1 for the squares of the cells. With every handle armed the same cells take 0.05 s, 0.62 s and 2.99 s: ratios of 12.4 and 4.8. Size alone does not set the time: the 85-handle function with one unarmed handle (5.46 MB) takes 5.25 s, and the all-armed 216 / 215 file (5.57 MB) takes 24.91 s.

**The unarmed handle multiplies it about 8 times.** The first call takes 0.36 s against 0.05 s at 43 handles (7.2 times), 5.25 s against 0.62 s at 85 (8.4 times) and 25.72 s against 2.99 s at 128 (8.6 times). At 85 handles the function is 59,008 lines against 15,578, and `snapshot.json` is 99.4 MB against 17.6 MB.

### Cause
grizzle's part: the function holds (participating tables) x (handles) `WHEN` arms (Code path 5), 16,512 at 128 handles, and with an unarmed handle one 8-line `IF NOT FOUND` refusal block per arm (Code path 3), against 129 refusals when every handle is armed. The `fixed` shape under Fix direction removes the second term and returns the same results. `unlessFound` is used outside `alternatives` only at `mirrors/index.ts:51`, where it is one probe per branch of constant size, so no other family multiplies in this way.

The measured facts:

- The cost is paid by the first call in a backend, and `CREATE FUNCTION` pays the same compile once (5.17 s for the 85-handle function with one unarmed handle). With `PGOPTIONS='-c check_function_bodies=off'`, `CREATE FUNCTION` of the same file takes 10.299 ms and its first `COMMIT` still takes 4,889.690 ms. The time is PL/pgSQL compilation of the body.
- The time follows the number of `WHEN` and `IF` statements, not the bytes (see the table).
- Two controls with no grizzle: a function of 10,000, 20,000 and 40,000 flat `PERFORM 1 FROM t WHERE id = x;` statements creates in 12.4, 24.6 and 47.6 ms, which is linear. A function with one `CASE` of 10,000, 20,000 and 40,000 `WHEN i THEN PERFORM 1;` arms creates in 640.014, 2,766.804 and 12,320.484 ms, which is quadratic.

Working theory for the quadratic term, traced in source and not profiled. PostgreSQL 19rc1 (tag `REL_19_RC1`) `pl_scanner.c` reads `/* be correct, but not fast, if input location goes backwards */` and calls `location_lineno_init`, which rescans the body line by line from the start.

https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/0b8bff042a997fe50fcd1c4c83b9ef1cc9ed8a89/src/pl/plpgsql/src/pl_scanner.c#L581-L583

Every `CASE ... WHEN` computes its line number after its body is parsed (`plpgsql_location_to_lineno`), and so does every `IF`.

https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/0b8bff042a997fe50fcd1c4c83b9ef1cc9ed8a89/src/pl/plpgsql/src/pl_gram.y#L1269

https://github.com/postgres/postgres/blob/0b8bff042a997fe50fcd1c4c83b9ef1cc9ed8a89/src/pl/plpgsql/src/pl_gram.y#L1196

Each `WHEN` and each `IF NOT FOUND` then pays one scan from the top. grizzle's two patches under [`scripts/pg/patches/`](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/tree/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/scripts/pg/patches) touch neither file.

### Impact
Every new connection's first law-checked write pays the compile, so a pool pays it once per connection: 0.36 s at 43 handles and 5.25 s at 85 with one unarmed handle, 0.62 s at 85 with every handle armed. `grizzle generate` writes a 99.4 MB `snapshot.json` for the 85-handle schema with one unarmed handle against 17.6 MB, and `DbLayer.make` takes that file when the app starts ([README.md:1062](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/README.md#L1062)).

### Not confirmed
- The `pl_scanner.c` rescan is the mechanism of the quadratic term. It fits the measured curve and the no-grizzle control, and was not profiled.
- Whether a patched grizzle emits the `fixed` shape and keeps the results. The fix direction was run on SQL produced by `gen-fix.sh`, not on a change to grizzle.
- Not run on an unpatched 19rc1; the two patches touch neither `src/pl` file.
- The cause of the stack overflow in `printPlBlock` at 123 to 128 handles with one unarmed handle.

### Fix direction
Possible change, tested on `gen-fix.sh`'s SQL and not on grizzle. The tested shape has one refusal per branch after `END CASE`, as the armed shape has, and `PERFORM 1;` in the `ELSE` so `FOUND` stays true. The raised error and its `DETAIL` are the same, and a schema with every handle armed generates the same text as today.

Five transactions at 2 handles and 2 branches (an unarmed parent alone; `k1` with no payload; `k1` with its payload; `k2` with only an `a1` payload; an unarmed parent plus a `k2` parent with its payload) end in COMMIT, ERROR, COMMIT, ERROR, COMMIT for `per-arm` and for the `fixed` shape. The same shape with an empty `ELSE` (`naive`) ends in ERROR, ERROR, COMMIT, ERROR, ERROR: it refuses an unarmed parent because `FOUND` is false at the first loop iteration, so the `PERFORM 1;` is needed. At 85 / 84 the `fixed` function is 15,469 lines and 895,644 bytes (1.1 percent more lines than the 15,301 of `trailing`) and its first `COMMIT` takes 0.62 s.

This removes only the constant factor of about 8, which answers the multiplier clause of the acceptance check. At 216 / 215 the `fixed` shape takes 24.41 s, against 24.91 s all armed, because the `CASE` is still repeated in every `TG_TABLE_NAME` branch.

A deeper direction, untested: a function that dispatched the `CASE` once per call would grow as tables + handles and not as their product. That would answer the growth clause.

### Acceptance check
On `grizzle generate` output of the schema of step 2, two clauses. Multiplier: the `ARMS=85 UNARMED=1` function is within 10 percent of the line count of the `UNARMED=0` function (15,578 lines), and the first `COMMIT` of the probe against it takes under twice as long as against the `UNARMED=0` function (0.62 s). Growth: the first `COMMIT` at `ARMS=128` takes under 3 times the first `COMMIT` at `ARMS=85` for the same `UNARMED` value (the cells grow 2.3 times, and the time grows about 5 times today).

---
**AI disclosure:** Written by AI, checked by independent AI reviewers, and filed without the account holder's review.
- **Provider:** Anthropic
- **Model:** Sonnet 5.5
````

Title: `grizzle check and generate fail with a RangeError in printPlBlock on an alternatives law with about 240 armed handles`

````text
### Summary
`grizzle check` and `grizzle generate` exit with `grizzle: internal error` and `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` at `printPlBlock` when one generated PL/pgSQL function is long enough. `printPlBlock` passes every line of the function body to one `Array.prototype.push` call as spread arguments. With a loop in place of that one call, on a patched copy of the installed `dist`, the same schema generates at the default stack and the output is identical (see Fix direction), so the cause is the argument count of that call and not the nesting depth. An `alternatives` law over a closed roster with every handle armed reaches it at 242 handles on Node 26.13.0 (macOS, arm64): 241 handles generate, 242 crash. At 242 handles the function's `BEGIN` to `END` span is 120,292 lines.

Ran `grizzle check` and `grizzle generate` on a standalone schema, one run per row. Read at `bjornpagen/grizzle@65203c4` (1.0.0-beta.0). The crash is at print time, before any SQL runs, so no database was used.

### Code path
1. `printPlBlock` joins the body into text. Its `BEGIN` line pushes `...plStatementsIndented(block.body)`, the whole body, as call arguments.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/sql/print.ts#L1029

2. The `alternatives` law function holds one `TG_TABLE_NAME` branch per participating table, and each branch holds the whole `CASE` with one `WHEN` per handle, so the body grows as the product of tables and handles. `dispatch` builds those branches as one `if` over the participating tables.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/residual/skeleton.ts#L205-L210

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/residual/skeleton.ts#L293-L302

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/laws/families/alternatives/index.ts#L44-L66

3. Every handle is an arm relation that also participates beside `Node`, so a roster of H handles has H + 1 participating tables and the function body is about 2·H·(H + 1) lines: 117,612 predicted at 242 handles, 120,292 measured. The nested spreads in the `if` and `case` printers take one `TG_TABLE_NAME` branch or one `WHEN` at a time, which holds a few lines per handle, so only the whole-body spread in step 1 reaches the stack limit.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/sql/print.ts#L960-L998

### Reproduction
1. Create a package on Node 26.13.0 (macOS, arm64; default `--stack-size` 984 KB) that depends on `@bjornpagen/grizzle` 1.0.0-beta.0 and `effect` 4.0.7. `@bjornpagen/grizzle` is not on npm (`npm view @bjornpagen/grizzle` returns 404), so both packages are packed from a clone at `65203c4` into the package's own `vendor/` folder (each `prepack` runs the build):

```sh
(cd packages/engine && pnpm pack --pack-destination <repro>/vendor)
(cd packages/grizzle && pnpm pack --pack-destination <repro>/vendor)
```

Then add two files and run `pnpm install`. `package.json` names both tarballs and `effect` 4.0.7. `pnpm-workspace.yaml` points the engine dependency at its tarball and holds `@effect/platform-node-shared` at 4.0.7, the version these runs used.

`package.json`:

```json
{
  "name": "repro",
  "private": true,
  "type": "module",
  "dependencies": {
    "@bjornpagen/grizzle": "file:vendor/bjornpagen-grizzle-1.0.0-beta.0.tgz",
    "effect": "4.0.7"
  }
}
```

`pnpm-workspace.yaml`:

```yaml
overrides:
  "@bjornpagen/grizzle-engine": file:vendor/bjornpagen-grizzle-engine-1.0.0-beta.0.tgz
  "@effect/platform-node-shared": 4.0.7
```

2. Save `schema.ts`, a closed roster of `ARMS` handles with an arm relation for each, and `grizzle.config.ts`.

```ts
import { alternatives, closed, closedId, login, relation, role, schema, uuid } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"

const arms = Number(process.env.ARMS ?? "3")
const handles = Array.from({ length: arms }, (_, i) => `k${i + 1}`)
export const Kind = closed("Kind", handles as [string, ...string[]])
export const Node = relation("Node", { id: uuid, kind: closedId(Kind) }, ["id"])
const armRelations = Object.fromEntries(handles.map((_, i) => [`A${i + 1}`, relation(`A${i + 1}`, { id: uuid }, ["id"])]))
const armMap = Object.fromEntries(handles.map((h, i) => [h, armRelations[`A${i + 1}`]]))
export const Repro = schema("Repro", { Kind, Node, ...armRelations } as any, [alternatives(Node, "kind", Kind, armMap as any)] as any)
const stored = { Node, ...armRelations } as any
export const Author = role(Repro, "author", stored, { write: Object.keys(stored) } as any)
export const App = login(Repro, "app", [Author], { workload: [] })
```

The four `as any` casts only type a roster built from `ARMS` at run time, which TypeScript cannot track as a tuple. grizzle imports `grizzle.config.ts` through Node's loader ([`main.ts#L53`](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/src/cli/main.ts#L53)), which strips the types, so the casts never reach the run, and the control below generates and checks the schema at 241 handles.

```ts
import { defineConfig } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"
import { App, Repro } from "./schema.ts"
export default defineConfig({ logins: [App], schemas: [Repro] })
```

3. Run `generate` and `check` with 241 handles, the control, then `generate` with 242. Every handle has an arm, so no handle is unarmed. The package starts with no `migrations` folder, and the control's folder is deleted before the 242 run.

Control, `ARMS=241`, default stack:

```console
$ ARMS=241 pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init
migrations/repro/20261102133627_init/01-setup.sql
migrations/repro/20261102133627_init/02-structure.sql
migrations/repro/20261102133627_init/03-validate.sql
migrations/repro/20261102133627_init/04-publish.sql
migrations/repro/20261102133627_init/meta.json
migrations/repro/snapshot.json
next: review the SQL, then run grizzle check
$ echo $?
0
$ ARMS=241 pnpm exec grizzle check
Repro: ok (1 migration, snapshot be594ac7e8e939df)
$ echo $?
0
$ rm -r migrations
```

Failure, `ARMS=242`, default stack. Paths shortened, otherwise verbatim, first lines of the trace.

```console
$ ARMS=242 pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init
grizzle: internal error
RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded
    at printPlBlock (.../grizzle-engine/dist/sql/print.js:1029:8)
    at createFunctionLines (.../grizzle-engine/dist/sql/print.js:1060:17)
    at statementLines (.../grizzle-engine/dist/sql/print.js:1256:11)
    at printStatement (.../grizzle-engine/dist/sql/print.js:1337:2)
    at emitText (.../grizzle-engine/dist/sql/emit.js:40:16)
    at emit (.../grizzle-engine/dist/sql/emit.js:50:14)
$ echo $?
1
$ ls migrations
ls: migrations: No such file or directory
```

`grizzle check` at `ARMS=242` fails the same way and exits 1. With `node --stack-size=3000 .../grizzle/dist/cli/main.js generate --name init` both sizes generate, and `03-validate.sql` is 120,546 lines at 241 handles and 121,530 at 242. The law function itself spans 120,292 lines at 242 handles, which sits between the 120,000 lines that a plain spread passes in step 4 and the 125,000 that it does not. The span is the first `BEGIN` line to the first `END` line, each alone on its line, in the `ARMS=242` file written under `--stack-size=3000`:

```sh
awk '/^BEGIN$/ && !b {b=NR} /^END$/ && b && !e {e=NR} END {print e-b+1}' migrations/repro/*/03-validate.sql
```

```text
120292
```

4. Control with no grizzle, a spread of generated strings into `push` at the default stack.

```js
const n = Number(process.argv[2])
const lines = Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => `line ${i}`)
const out = []
try {
  out.push("BEGIN", ...lines, "END")
  console.log(`spread of ${n} lines: ok`)
} catch (e) {
  console.log(`spread of ${n} lines: ${e.name}: ${e.message}`)
}
```

```text
spread of 120000 lines: ok
spread of 125000 lines: RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded
spread of 150000 lines: RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded
```

With `node --stack-size=2000` the 150,000-line spread is ok.

### Expected
A schema that the README's Limits admit is printed, or refused with a named refusal. The Limits list names no ceiling on the handles of a closed roster or on the size of a generated function, and names other limits, such as 100,000 rows per ChangeSet input, in the same list. Here the process exits with `grizzle: internal error`.

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/README.md#L1881-L1899

### What changes the result
Each row is `grizzle check` on the schema above with every handle armed, in a package with no `migrations` folder, run as `ARMS=<handles> node --stack-size=<KB> node_modules/@bjornpagen/grizzle/dist/cli/main.js check` (the default stack, 984 KB, needs no flag). A crash is `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` at `printPlBlock`. A finished run prints `Repro: the schemas or their logins differ from snapshot.json (an ungenerated change); run grizzle generate`, because the folder has no migrations. The last column is the smallest size measured that finishes; the next size down that was run crashed, at 986, 1120 and 2100 KB for 242, 260 and 363 handles. Measured on one machine (macOS arm64, Node 26.13.0).

| Handles | Result at the default stack (984 KB) | Smallest `--stack-size` that prints (KB) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 241 | prints | 984 |
| 242 | RangeError | 988 |
| 260 | RangeError | 1140 |
| 363 | RangeError | 2150 |

With the loop of Fix direction in place of `print.js:1029` in a copy of the installed `dist`, the same schema at 242, 300 and 363 handles runs `check` at the default stack with no crash, and `generate` at 242 exits 0. The unpatched rows above fail at the stack size that a plain spread of that many arguments also fails at (step 4), which is the mechanism: the argument count of the one call, not the nesting.

### Impact
On Node 26.13.0 (macOS, arm64) at the default stack, `grizzle check` and `grizzle generate` fail on an `alternatives` law from 242 armed handles. The handle count at which they fail moves with the stack size (table above).

The schema this was found on has a closed roster of 216 handles in one part, every handle armed, and an estimated 363 once its parts are composed, so 242 handles is inside the sizes it meets.

### Not confirmed
- Whether `grizzle migrate` fails at the same size. Read in source, not run: its drift check prints through the same `emit` ([`drift.ts#L337`](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/src/adapter/admission/drift.ts#L337)), and no database was used.
- Whether another spread in the printer fails at sizes past 363 handles.
- The stack size at which other Node versions fail. Node 26.13.0 only.

### Fix direction
Possible change to `packages/engine/src/sql/print.ts` at line 1029, tested on a patched copy of the installed `dist/sql/print.js` (the same line) and not on grizzle's repository. Pushing the body in a loop instead of one spread call leaves the function text unchanged and removes the argument limit:

```diff
-	lines.push("BEGIN", ...plStatementsIndented(block.body), "END")
+	lines.push("BEGIN")
+	for (const line of plStatementsIndented(block.body)) lines.push(line)
+	lines.push("END")
```

Reference first: the unpatched CLI under a larger stack writes the folder to compare against, in a package with no `migrations` folder. The change above goes into the installed `dist/sql/print.js` after `mv migrations unpatched`, and the patched copy then runs at the default stack; its folder is compared with the reference:

```console
$ ARMS=242 node --stack-size=3000 node_modules/@bjornpagen/grizzle/dist/cli/main.js generate --name init
migrations/repro/20261102144218_init/01-setup.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144218_init/02-structure.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144218_init/03-validate.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144218_init/04-publish.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144218_init/meta.json
migrations/repro/snapshot.json
next: review the SQL, then run grizzle check
$ mv migrations unpatched
$ ARMS=242 pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init
migrations/repro/20261102144240_init/01-setup.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144240_init/02-structure.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144240_init/03-validate.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144240_init/04-publish.sql
migrations/repro/20261102144240_init/meta.json
migrations/repro/snapshot.json
next: review the SQL, then run grizzle check
$ echo $?
0
$ ARMS=242 pnpm exec grizzle check
Repro: ok (1 migration, snapshot a83a474bc865ee65)
$ echo $?
0
$ diff -r unpatched/repro/*_init migrations/repro/*_init; echo $?
0
$ diff unpatched/repro/snapshot.json migrations/repro/snapshot.json; echo $?
0
```

The two migration folders differ only in the timestamp in their names, so the SQL, `meta.json` and the snapshot are byte-identical.

### Acceptance check
After the change, at the default stack, `ARMS=242 pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init` exits 0, `ARMS=242 pnpm exec grizzle check` then prints `Repro: ok (1 migration, ...)` and exits 0, and `ARMS=363 pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init` exits 0 in a package with no `migrations` folder. The folder written for `ARMS=242` is byte-identical to the one the CLI before the change writes under `--stack-size=3000`, apart from the timestamp in its name.

### Related
[#5](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/issues/5) reports this stack overflow at 123 to 128 handles with one unarmed handle and lists its cause under Not confirmed. The line counts in #5 fit the same ceiling. Computed from #5's line counts, not measured: with one unarmed handle the function has about 8.07 lines per table-by-handle cell (59,008 lines over 7,310 cells at 85 handles), against about 2.1 with every handle armed (15,578 over 7,310 there, and 120,292 over 58,806 at 242 handles here). The function therefore reaches the ceiling, the one shown above (the 120,292-line all-armed function crashes at 242 handles, and in step 4 a spread of 120,000 passes while 125,000 does not), with one unarmed handle between `ARMS=120` (about 117,000 lines, which #5 says runs) and `ARMS=123` (about 123,000 lines, which #5 says crashes).

#5 itself is about the PostgreSQL compile time of the first call. Its `fixed` shape changes the law's text but keeps the all-armed line count, so it leaves this crash.

- **Model:** Sonnet 5.5
````

### Draft: the rename-witness crash (row 50)

Drafted by hand `e5984d51` and filed as [bjornpagen/grizzle#8](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/issues/8). Title: check and generate: internal error when two removed relations pair with one added relation that renames a field.

````text
### Summary
`grizzle check` and `grizzle generate` exit 1 with `grizzle: internal error` and `grizzle internal invariant: a statement of its own for candidate 0` when one added relation could be a rename of two or more removed relations, and each of those offers the same nested field rename onto it. Nothing is drafted or written, no flag or marker gets past it, and the error names no relation or field. The same schema change drafts normally when only one relation was removed.

Ran `@bjornpagen/grizzle` 1.0.0-beta.0, packed from `bjornpagen/grizzle@65203c4`, with `effect` 4.0.7, Node v26.13.0, pnpm 12.16.1, macOS arm64.

### Reproduction
The package is not on npm, so both packages were installed from `pnpm pack` tarballs of `packages/grizzle` and `packages/engine` at `65203c4`; the project files are in the fold below.

`schema.v1.ts` holds two relations of one shape, each keyed by `node`, each with a field named `value`:

```ts
import { pg, relation, schema, uuid } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"

export const First = relation("First", { node: uuid, value: pg.bigint }, ["node"])
export const Second = relation("Second", { node: uuid, value: pg.bigint }, ["node"])

export const Repro = schema("Repro", { First, Second }, [])
```

`schema.v2-fail.ts` removes both and adds one relation keyed by `node` whose other field is named `amount`:

```ts
import { pg, relation, schema, uuid } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"

export const Third = relation("Third", { amount: pg.bigint, node: uuid }, ["node"])

export const Repro = schema("Repro", { Third }, [])
```

With `schema.v1.ts` copied to `schema.ts`, `pnpm exec grizzle generate --name init` drafts the first migration and exits 0. Then:

```console
$ cp schema.v2-fail.ts schema.ts
$ pnpm exec grizzle check
grizzle: internal error
Error: grizzle internal invariant: a statement of its own for candidate 0
    at defect (<pnpm>/engine/dist/core/invariant.js:29:48)
    at <pnpm>/engine/dist/deploy/render/generate.js:150:10
    at catchSignal (<pnpm>/engine/dist/core/canonical.js:147:20)
    ...
$ echo $?
1
$ pnpm exec grizzle generate --name next
grizzle: internal error
Error: grizzle internal invariant: a statement of its own for candidate 0
    ...
$ echo $?
1
$ ls migrations/repro
20261103194532_init
snapshot.json
```

Traces shortened to three frames; pnpm store paths shortened to `<pnpm>`, otherwise verbatim.

<details>
<summary>Project files: package.json, pnpm-workspace.yaml, grizzle.config.ts</summary>

```sh
(cd packages/engine && pnpm pack --pack-destination <repro>/vendor)
(cd packages/grizzle && pnpm pack --pack-destination <repro>/vendor)
```

`package.json`:

```json
{
  "name": "repro",
  "private": true,
  "type": "module",
  "dependencies": {
    "@bjornpagen/grizzle": "file:vendor/bjornpagen-grizzle-1.0.0-beta.0.tgz",
    "effect": "4.0.7"
  }
}
```

`pnpm-workspace.yaml` (the `@effect/platform-node-shared` pin keeps the install on `effect` 4.0.7):

```yaml
packages: []
overrides:
  "@bjornpagen/grizzle-engine": "file:vendor/bjornpagen-grizzle-engine-1.0.0-beta.0.tgz"
  "@effect/platform-node-shared": "4.0.7"
```

`grizzle.config.ts`:

```ts
import { defineConfig } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle"
import { Repro } from "./schema.ts"

export default defineConfig({ schemas: [Repro], logins: [] })
```

</details>

**Control.** The same `schema.v2-fail.ts` over a snapshot with one removed relation. `schema.v1-one.ts` declares `First` alone, with the same line as above. `check` reports the ungenerated change and `generate` drafts:

```console
$ pnpm exec grizzle check
Repro: the schemas or their logins differ from snapshot.json (an ungenerated change); run grizzle generate
$ pnpm exec grizzle generate --name next
migrations/repro/20261103194602_next/01-structure.sql
migrations/repro/20261103194602_next/02-publish.sql
migrations/repro/20261103194602_next/meta.json
migrations/repro/snapshot.json
```

In that draft, line 3 is the relation rename and line 7 is the nested field rename. The field rename names the added table, `third`:

```sql
-- ALTER TABLE "repro"."first" RENAME TO "third";
-- -- ALTER TABLE "repro"."third" RENAME COLUMN "value" TO "amount";
```

### What changes the result
Each row starts from a snapshot of the removed relations and applies one added relation.

| Removed relations | Added relation | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `First`, `Second`, each `{ node, value: bigint }` | `Third { amount: bigint, node }` | exit 1, internal error |
| `First`, `Second`, each `{ node, value: bigint }` | `Third { amount: integer, node }` | exit 1, internal error (retype path) |
| `First { node, value: bigint }` alone | `Third { amount: bigint, node }` | drafts (the Control) |
| `First { node, value: bigint }`, `Second { node, other: bigint }` | `Third { amount: bigint, node }` | drafts |

Row 2 replaces `Third` with `export const Third = relation("Third", { amount: pg.integer, node: uuid }, ["node"])`; row 4 replaces `Second` with `export const Second = relation("Second", { node: uuid, other: pg.bigint }, ["node"])`.

### Cause
Read at `65203c4`.

1. Each relation candidate builds its nested field candidates (`renameCandidateColumn`, `retypeCandidateColumn`) before it pushes itself, so they are numbered first: candidate 0 is the first nested field candidate.
   https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/diff/diff.ts#L2978-L2996
   https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/diff/diff.ts#L3020
2. A nested candidate's statement is `renameColumn` on the added table, from the removed field name to the added field name. It does not mention the removed relation, so the text is identical across rival relation candidates. The retype path builds the same statement.
   https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/diff/diff.ts#L3179
   https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/diff/diff.ts#L3231

   When the removed field names differ, the two texts differ and the draft succeeds:

   ```sql
   -- ALTER TABLE "repro"."first" RENAME TO "third";
   -- -- ALTER TABLE "repro"."third" RENAME COLUMN "value" TO "amount";
   -- ALTER TABLE "repro"."second" RENAME TO "third";
   -- -- ALTER TABLE "repro"."third" RENAME COLUMN "other" TO "amount";
   ```
3. `witnessesOf` takes a statement as a witness only when no other candidate's offer holds the same text. Every nested candidate's only statement is held by its rival's nested candidate, so none qualifies, and `witnessesOf` returns the index of the first candidate without a witness, here candidate 0.
   https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/render/chosen.ts#L60-L64
   https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/render/chosen.ts#L75-L78

The relation candidates are fine: each owns `ALTER TABLE "<removed>" RENAME TO "<added>"`, which differs by removed name.

### Expected
The error is an internal `defect`, not a refusal, and neither the README's Limits section nor the agent guide lists this input. The comments at the throw site and in `witnessesOf` say a draft never offers a candidate without a witness of its own, and the agent guide documents field candidates nested in a relation's rename as supported:

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/render/generate.ts#L143-L151

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/engine/src/deploy/render/chosen.ts#L48-L51

https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/blob/65203c443fa969cc47c539666a998436595f42fe/packages/grizzle/skills/grizzle/SKILL.md#L162-L165

### Not confirmed
- What `meta.json` would hold for the two nested candidates. The draft throws before `meta.json` is written, so the identical `label` and `renames` of the colliding candidates were read in the source and not observed.
- Whether any other nested candidate kind collides across rival relation candidates. Only the same-type and retype field paths were read and run.

### Fix direction
A possible change, untested: tie each nested field candidate to its parent relation candidate, so candidates nested under rival relation renames get distinct witnesses, labels and `renames` in `meta.json`. Rival relation candidates for one added name already cannot both be chosen.

### Acceptance check
Over the two-relation snapshot and `schema.v2-fail.ts`, `pnpm exec grizzle generate --name next` exits 0 and drafts a rename of `first` and of `second` to `third`, each with its nested field rename.
````

### Considered and not filed

The `K.handle` row check (row 17) was drafted as a defect and then withdrawn: the step-3 independent check (auditor `1fa90013`, Opus 5.5, 2026-11-02) found it a documented limit, not a grizzle defect, because `Operand` is documented as a term of exactly `T` or a literal and the handle's narrow type is deliberate, so nothing was filed as a defect. It was then filed as a documentation gap, [bjornpagen/grizzle#7](https://github.com/bjornpagen/grizzle/issues/7) (label `documentation`, operator-approved, filed by the overseer as `aidenzepp`): it asks for one README sentence under "Constants are typed by where they go" (`README.md:1392-1393`) and says the types should not change; grizzle's own `test/support/worlds/model/types.ts:169-170` answered the question version. A change request to widen `Operand` to accept a term of any single member of `T` remains the operator's call and is not filed.

### M3 (feature processing, `p3-34`)

Triaged against grizzle's source at `65203c4` and not recorded as rows, with the finding each came from.

- **No refusal, no row.** `p3-01` (`2370328`): `relation(...)` accepted `identifierList`, `toleranceList` and `coords`, with no `charLength` fallback. `p3-02` (`9cdaec7`): all eleven probe relations over the reference unions and the eight enumerations construct; no regex-budget refusal. `p3-19` (`1e51a75`): no marker (only the `-- grizzle: no-transaction` headers). `p3-21` (`ca61eaa`): `grizzle migrate` applied six migrations in 37.7 s with no refusal and the stage `statement` never reached. `p3-30` (no commit): no program was generated (row 25's budget). Sizes, not refusals: `05-validate.sql` of the `processing` folder is 4,557,273 bytes (the `node_kind` alternatives law function, 176 trigger bodies), `snapshot.json` 76,867,094 bytes at the freeze.
- **Our misuse, fixed or left to a repair slice.** `p3-20` (`f54a308`): `Compute.cond` refused as a check predicate (`MalformedDescription`). A row check's helpers are `ScalarHelpers`, the comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`, `in`, `between` and `charLength` (`packages/engine/src/types/compute/compute.ts:301` to `305`), and `cond` is a term constructor whose first branch is a term (`:200` to `204`); P8 and P9 are written as `or`, `and`, `in` and `eq` predicates. The five placeholder markers of `p3-20` were the expected ones, resolved with the constants the brief names. `p3-33` (`d463047`): P3's witness maps to `document-key` because `keyRules` names `RuleOperand.(child)` and the relation's primary key is `(rule)`: the law is a `primaryKey` law whose key witness names `RuleOperand` (`rule`), so the map could name P3; a gap in `src/import/refusal.ts`, a repair there. An `Operand` with no matching `Edge`, and `ResponseIfArm.condition` (S2), map to `condition-edge` and not to a P name: `processingContainedRule` places both under the edge-bound arms; also `src/import/refusal.ts`. `p3-32` (`9923b94`): six causes outside grizzle (shadows table, unmodeled vocabulary, a fixture's required attribute, the item identifier, the guards not wired, `p49` not an `ImportRule`), repaired by `p3-40`, `p3-41`, `p3-42` and `p3-32.1`.
- **Rows added above.** The type checker (bin 2's class), the pattern law's witness (P7 against `value-lexical`) and the single law of a refusal. P28, P30, P31 and P32 extend the rows of count over a union of kinds (row 13), sibling order (row 11), reachability (row 12) and the typed and text copies (rows 8 and 9) through their `Met in` cells; `p3-30` extends row 25's.

### M4 (tests, sections, stimuli; `p5-54`, `p5-55`)

Triaged against grizzle `48256f8` and not recorded as rows beyond the cells above.

- **No grizzle defect.** Every one of the 53 reds of the first leased run (merge `3e88b8af`) and the later reds (decisions 289, 292, 293 and 294: the `stimulus-300-include` fixture, the `Picture` rule naming, the paired merges, the importer's external-metadata pass) was ours: fixtures, tests, the importer, the refusal naming.
