The one cause that touched a grizzle behaviour, `Picture`'s `img` capacity refusing every stimulus package (cause B), is row 48's documented limit, handled there. The dry run `p5-54` and both generates of `p5-55` met no `ResourceBound`, and the full suite with the corpus passed 3385 of 3385 under merge `db0619aa`. Run handles: `3e88b8af`, `db0619aa`.

### M2f (feature wildcard, `p11-17`)

Triaged against grizzle's source at `48256f8` and not recorded as rows, with the finding each came from.

- **No refusal, no row.** `p11-14` (`19685d1`): `grizzle generate --name wildcard` ran 33.8 s at a peak of 1.64 GB, with no printer crash, no `ResourceBound` and no marker (enum labels drafted as `ADD VALUE`, nothing to resolve); `48256f8` has no program size cap (decision 282), so no printer ceiling met this phase. `p11-15`, `p11-16` (`0b79968`): the full suite on server A ran 3,205 tests with none failed, and the 21 database cases of `src/schema/foreign.db.test.ts` passed, the W9 and W10 negatives refused by `primaryKey ForeignAttribute(node,prefix,local)`, `primaryKey DocumentXmlId(document,id)` and `primaryKey ManifestId(package,id)`.
- **Our misuse, fixed here.** The first leased run (merge `badb4482`, decision 295) had ten reds and every one was ours: a `bigint` mixed with a `number` in `foreign.db.test.ts`, a fallback with no rule naming its law in `src/import/refusal.ts`, and four tests whose expected counts and seed headers the new migration made stale. W9's key on the prefix and not on the namespace is our model's choice, forced by the byte bound above, and is not a finding; the spec row W9 states the namespace, so the disagreement is the integration plan's.
- **The probe's method.** `src/schema/probe.foreign.test.ts` asserts what `schema(...)` accepts. `schema(...)` accepted forms that `grizzle check` refuses (the byte-bound row and the policy row above), so a probe is a first filter, never the verdict, and a later probe states the stage it ran at. Not grizzle's: the `schema(...)` call is the declaration and the refusal comes at compile, as the row on a containment into a selected target already records. Form (f), a selected-source capacity of `within(0n, 0n)`, built at `schema(...)` and no statement of the final model uses it, so it was never compiled and no verdict is claimed.
- **Not grizzle findings.** The `libxml2` 3.0.1 disagreement (spec 3.3, lax/09) is an oracle note for GOAL 7.4. L-1 (a complex `xsi:type` on lax foreign content is not checked), L-2 (`xs:ENTITY` is not verified) and L-3 (an unjudged simple `xsi:type` is checked for shape) are QTI-model limits (decision 227, reading 50), not statements grizzle cannot make. The two seed folders of the wildcard migration differ from the printer in comments only, because `seed_wildcard` was generated with `--minus migrations/qti` (decision 299): our tooling.

## W5: the rows-first cutover (grizzle `4016b69`, checked at trunk `46b2fc3`)

W5 moved `packages/pantry` onto the rows-first schema at grizzle trunk `4016b69`
(`4016b692ffd1493d72b752fc5ad85cc37df0fc95`), the interim pin of `0f6264a`. Its findings were triaged against
grizzle's source at that pin and re-checked at trunk `46b2fc3` (`46b2fc396d8e9023bd54b6e1a2b9712a755a67f4`), grizzle
`main`'s HEAD on 2026-11-06, where the local `main` and `origin/main` agreed. That tree also held the grizzle seats'
uncommitted fixes, named below where they apply. None of these findings was filed: the swarm's grizzle seats fixed every
defect but one in grizzle's own tree, and that one, the empty-language admission check, is assigned with the pg-tier
fixes, so no issue went through the gate.

### A fold over unbounded text was capped at PostgreSQL's btree tuple size (defect, fixed in `e65462a` and `c0da11b`)

- **Found by:** W5 Q5.03, `Node.stringValue`, a `Monoid.concat` fold over the node tree, at grizzle `4016b69`.
- **What happened:** a fold's before and after accumulators ride the family's predecessor and last axes. They join
  the axes' UNIQUE superkey and the foreign keys, so the text accumulator is a btree index key. PostgreSQL 19rc1 caps
  an index tuple at `BTMaxItemSize`, 2,704 bytes at BLCKSZ 8192 (`src/include/access/nbtree.h:165`, enforced by
  `_bt_check_third_page`, `nbtutils.c:1118-1150`, SQLSTATE 54000), on the compressed tuple (`index_form_tuple`
  compresses past `TOAST_INDEX_TARGET`, about 510 bytes, `indextuple.c:121`). So the schema compiled, and a group whose
  running text passed about 2.6 kB of incompressible data was refused at insert.
- **Classification:** first recorded as a documented limit (README, key representability). It is a grizzle defect: a
  declared key is held to the B-tree entry rule, and a family axis's UNIQUE was not (`laws/model/plan.ts`). GF fixed
  it in `e65462a` and `c0da11b` ("Every axis key fits its B-tree entry"): an axis past the bound is now refused when
  the schema is compiled with `UnrepresentableKey`, naming the UNIQUE, the family and the accumulator, and the README
  gains the Limits bullet "Every key fits its index entry".
- **qti-pie's workaround, kept after the fix:** the fold's step and accumulators are `keyText` (`src/qti/limits.ts`,
  648 code points since Node's fold), and only text a law checks or an overlay copies is folded
  (`src/qti/node.ts`, `inStringValue`); a longer value is refused by the representation limit's law, never by a QTI
  law's name. The corpus's longest simple-content value is 967 B, a description no law reads, so nothing folds it.
- **Possible grizzle change:** carry a digest of a text accumulator in the axes, with the text stored and proved
  locally by the fold's CHECK. Not filed; it is a design question for the operator.

### Other defects fixed in grizzle during W5

- **`closed.relation` refused an identical repeated row** as a second row of its key (`ir/desc.ts:1832` against
  `schema/api.ts:221` at `4016b69`). Fixed by GF in `c0da11b`: a closed relation's rows are a set, a repeat states
  one row with the same digest and row count, and the same key with another payload is still `MalformedDescription`.
- **G1: a law column over a selected generated side read a generated column**, and PostgreSQL refused the
  installation's `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` with 42P17 (`physical/ddl/tables.ts:741`). Fixed by GT in `80f77bd`
  (`lowerWrittenRow`: the law column reads the field's own generation), finished in `bf1f470`.
- **G2: a cast past the DFA bounds was reported as `PartialOperation`.** Fixed by GT in `561e4c7`, documented in
  `80f77bd`: a language inclusion past 2,048 DFA states or 2,097,152 NFA-state visits is `ResourceBound` with the bound
  `regexInclusion`.
- **`Bits.of` as a set operation's second operand was typed but refused by the builder** (`builders.ts:324`). Fixed
  by GW in `80f77bd`: the second operand may be a set value over the first's roster, a literal that takes its type.
- **A put or delete of a member keyed by a drawn field hit an internal invariant** (`normalize.ts:251`,
  `edit.ts:138`). Fixed by GW in `80f77bd` (`UnstatedKey`, a `CompileError` naming the key and the unstated fields)
  and extended to declared candidate keys in `e25c2f5`.
- **`matches` in a query over unbounded text could fail past 268,435,454 B** (`regexp.c:332`: the regex engine
  widens its subject to four bytes a byte, past `MaxAllocSize`). Fixed by GT in `e25c2f5`: a query matches or collapses
  only a text whose pattern bounds it within that limit (`regexTextMaxBytes`), and otherwise is `UnsupportedOperation`.
- **A generated term could read an accumulated field by type and failed at run time with a misleading message**
  (`schema/api.ts:162`, `relation.ts:452`, `terms.ts:78-107`; Q5.03). Fixed by GW in `46b2fc3`: a generation reads the
  fields its caller writes alone, by type and by `GeneratedRefused` (`readsUnwritten`).
- **A nested tree member's excess keys typechecked** (`types/changeset/changeset.ts:86-145`; Q5.03), and a stated drawn
  or accumulated value at any depth was dropped unsaid. Fixed by GW in `46b2fc3`: a type error through every nesting,
  and `EncodingFailure` (`factShape`) from JavaScript.
- **Fixed in grizzle's tree, uncommitted at `46b2fc3`,** found by grizzle's own suite during the W5 gate: power's
  integer-digit bounds under-counted `x^0`, `1^y` and `(10^-s)^-y` (`scalar/compute/lower.ts`, `powerBounds`; GSW);
  `write/frontend/schedule.ts` counted a pure set's put as a removal, which put the referencing insert first in an
  immediate foreign-key cycle (GSW); the span producers claimed `certified` sets over inputs that may repeat (GSS,
  `ProducerForm`); and a field rename left a constraint over the old column name in later snapshots, so Validate's drop
  ordering failed with 2BP01 (`deploy/diff/declared.ts`; GMG).
- **Open: the empty-language admission check misses mid-pattern anchors.** `^$a` is admitted and lowered to `\A\Za`,
  which no text matches, where `matchPattern` refuses only a pattern whose IR is `never` (`scalar/regex/admit.ts:121`
  at `46b2fc3`; found by PG). A small defect, assigned with the pg-tier fixes; not filed.

### Limits met during W5, with qti-pie's workarounds

Each is grizzle behaving as designed. Where the README at `46b2fc3` states it, its section is named; the rest are
limits the README does not yet state, recorded as limits.

- **A 23502 from a generated `NOT NULL` column names no law** (not in the README). Q5.05 states AL1's admission as a
  named check, `AL1_admission`, beside the generated column, so the refusal names its law.
- **`db.explain` drops index names** (not in the README, whose "Reading" says it returns the estimated plans as
  data), so the plan audits assert node types (Q5.16).
- **A CHECK's or a foreign key's refusal has the witness `unreported`** ("Errors and outcomes"). The importer takes an
  injected locator (`ImportOptions.locate`): tests locate the row by the law oracle's verdicts (`src/test/locate.ts`),
  production keeps `unreported` and says the database names no row, and `ImportRefused` carries the witness kind for
  the command line (Q5.15, Q5.17, PS).
- **A nested family's rows keep the nesting's order** ("Families": an ordered member's "order is the nesting's"), so the regex-cache ordering of a statement's rows by
  pattern input reaches flat relations only (Q5.15). See the ideas below.
- **`db.apply` compiles its ChangeSet for the call** ("Reading": a program outside the workloads is compiled "for the
  call", and `db.compile` compiles a ChangeSet once for reuse; Q5.15). The importer builds one ChangeSet per package
  from that package's rows, so there is nothing to reuse, and it pays one compile per import.
- **A flat relation is never split across statements,** so one transaction holds a column only below 536,870,888
  characters (`write/program/bind.ts:559`; "Errors and outcomes" names the bound and says to split the input across
  calls). The importer writes one package in one transaction and does not split (Q5.20).
- **A pattern whose language is empty is refused** (not in the README; PG item 8), so NOTATION's empty value domain is
  stated as a domain-identity check (`emptyDomains`; PX).
- **Lookaround is outside the pattern grammar** (`str.regex`'s, which "Queries" names without listing what it leaves
  out), so QX's pattern emitter writes none.
- **There were no translate, case-fold or whitespace-collapse terms.** GT added `Compute.translate` and `lowerAscii`
  (`80f77bd`) and `collapse` and `replaceWhitespace` (`e25c2f5`); before them PI compared tags exactly and trimmed
  `xs:token` names by hand.
- **A literal cannot be a first operand** (G3; "Queries": "Its first operand is a term, as every operation's is").
  A leading constant is written as a typed literal term, `Compute.literal(T, …)`.
- **A family's member side cannot be a selection** (`analysis/theory/families.ts:166-174`, not in the README; IN,
  Q5.13): the in-place model keys its families by owner and states the selection as a containment beside them.
- **A side's terms must be distinct** (`MalformedDescription`, not in the README; IN).
- **A relation parameter whose pattern no stored column carries cannot be delivered** (`UnsupportedOperation`; GMG).
  GMG's README Limits bullet ("A parameter's value of a pattern is delivered only through that pattern's domain") is
  in grizzle's tree, uncommitted at `46b2fc3`.

### Environment: plpgsql_check's profiler PANICs a COMMIT once its shared store is full (not grizzle's)

- **Where:** server B, which preloads plpgsql_check 2.10.15 with `plpgsql_check.profiler = on` and `max_stats_size =
  200MB`. AGENTS.md says that profiler is off; that sentence is stale. Found by GF, 2026-11-06.
- **What the profiler does:** at transaction end `lxcache_reset_callback` (`src/profiler.c:858`) merges each
  function's statistics into two shared hashes with `hash_search(..., HASH_ENTER)` (`profiler.c:1335`, and about
  `:1095`), from a callback on a child of TopTransactionContext. Both hashes are fixed at `FUNC_STATS_COUNT` = 20000
  entries (`profiler.c:102`, `:365`, `:369`).
- **How the store fills:** an entry is keyed by (fn_oid, db_oid, fn_xmin, fn_tid), and dropping a function never
  removes it, so the store fills after 20,000 function identities, which a suite that installs fresh schemas reaches.
- **What a full store does:** dynahash raises a hintless `ERROR: out of shared memory` (`dynahash.c:1027`). At COMMIT
  that error comes after the commit record (`xact.c` `CommitTransaction`: `RecordTransactionCommit`, then
  `AtCommit_Memory`), so it ends in `PANIC: cannot abort transaction …, it was already committed` (`xact.c:1845`) and a
  crash restart. The `max_stats_size` WARNING that grizzle's `scripts/pg/init.sh:74` relies on guards the statement
  slots, not the hash entries, and the `if (!fss) elog(ERROR …)` at `profiler.c:1263` is dead code, because
  `HASH_ENTER` never returns NULL.
- **Seen:** B crashed this way at 04:13:29 and 05:25:03, each about 70 minutes after shared memory was fresh (a crash
  restart empties the store), at 6 and at 3 workers alike, so the trigger is cumulative, not parallelism.
- **Upstream:** fixed in okbob/plpgsql_check `4ac5df4a99` (2026-10-28, `HASH_ENTER_NULL` and a WARNING), on master
  after v2.10.16; no release has it yet.
- **Workaround:** before each harness on B, `CREATE EXTENSION plpgsql_check` in a scratch database `gz_profreset`, call
  `plpgsql_profiler_reset_all()` (it HASH_REMOVEs every entry of both hashes and zeroes `used_stmt_stats_count`,
  `profiler.c` about `:470`–`:505`), then drop that database; no server configuration changes. Re-pinning
  plpgsql_check past `4ac5df4a99` removes the crash.

### Ideas for the operator (not filed)

- **A law's identity drops a side's selection,** so two laws over selections of one relation can share a name
  (Q5.17); the refusal table tells them apart by hand.
- **Witness a CHECK refusal on the host,** so a refusal names its row without a caller's locator (Q5.15).
- **A shared discriminant index for selection keys,** if Attribute's UNIQUEs cost too much at N1 (Q5.07).
- **Order a family statement's rows by their pattern input,** so a nested family meets PostgreSQL's 32-regex cache in
  groups, as flat relations can (Q5.15).
- **Make `Written` and `AdditionIn` public** (Q5.15), so a builder can name the row type it writes.
- **A role's description costs as much as its schema:** `role(Qti, "author", …)` over Qti's 137 stored relations
  took 2.0 s and 271 MB when its module loaded (measured 2026-11-06 at a load average of 27, after the schema
  itself, 1.6 s and 63 MB). qti-pie now builds it only where it opens a database (PS); whether the description can be
  built lazily is grizzle's question.

## The final pin (grizzle `99b6217`, then `64f0427`, `f66c05b`, `a2ff5eb`, `fce1088`, `4b155e8`, `27c4cd2`, `dec8ab3` and `1bf3dbb`)

qti-pie was pinned to grizzle trunk `99b6217` (`99b621761896c5537f4d24d5ba4f23b9eb97bf15`), the tip that carries the pg
tier's fix wave, and then to `64f0427` (`64f0427a629ffde66c6d73d5b4ca596fe14001b7`), which fixes the defect below. The
findings were triaged against `99b6217`'s source and re-checked at trunk `7c58b10`
(`7c58b1045e47ed9282cebd1ecadc9b68c64765b0`), which changes only grizzle's `TODO.md`, and then at `64f0427`, `main`'s
HEAD on 2026-11-06. At each pin two exports of the commit packed the three tarballs to the same bytes
(`vendor/README.md`); between the two, only the engine's tarball changed. On 2026-11-06 qti-pie moved to `f66c05b`
(`f66c05b71bb016247163520f29d69de516968c2c`), which grants a role what its writes evaluate through casts and domains,
writes a tree that owns an ordered family, decides schema-only write refusals when the schema compiles, adds
`Compute.octetLength` and the public `keyTextBytes(fields)`, and cuts startup to one compile per installation. Two
exports of it packed identical tarballs, and its testing tarball and its skill are byte-identical to `64f0427`'s. Then
qti-pie moved to `a2ff5eb` (`a2ff5eb494af2e0f89024c1e90f3a19d7cbee6b3`, its repin `b9dcadbb`), where hex is the only
closed-relation form (the rows form and the `{ pack }` option are deleted, and a set is rounded to whole hex digits),
closed relations are admitted once, a take's order is fixed at the root, and a contract's facts are its declared
`proves`. pantry deleted its seven `{ pack: "hex" }` arguments (`src/grammar/install.ts`) and needed no `proves`. Two
exports packed identical tarballs; the testing tarball is unchanged, and the shipped skill changed, its closed-relation
constant now always one hex literal. Then it moved to `fce1088` (`fce10882b08165ebb44125fc0c9ddac6572c6495`, its repin
`8439df91`), where a key's text budget counts the null bitmap: every B-tree entry is sized from the data offset after
one (the engine's `src/schema/representability.ts:793`). Two exports packed identical tarballs, and the testing tarball
and the skill are unchanged. The `Qti` schema does not yet compile at this pin, for a containment's reverse index that
refuses an unbounded source column, which grizzle's lane K fixed in `4b155e8` ("A containment's reverse index refuses a
source column of no byte bound", below). Then qti-pie moved to `4b155e8` (`4b155e8a88860a5cac544f38fe664f33d43a0a98`),
its repin `a60eb2c1`, where a containment's referencing width never refuses it and only the referenced UNIQUE must be
bounded (the reverse-index entry below). Two exports packed identical tarballs; all three changed, the testing tarball
in its `src/pattern.ts`, and the shipped skill changed (a contract's facts are exactly its `proves`). Two copy-backs,
`ExpressionAttribute`'s and `SectionIdentifier`'s, referenced a UNIQUE over the unbounded `Attribute.lexical`. That was
our misuse, against C18's own rule, and each is now stated over its selection's key, the law unchanged (`70a76ff3`,
whose message gives the proof). The single `_init` was regenerated at this pin (`5ca47304`, `20261106234911`, snapshot
`61c625ec…`): no `string_agg` and no `VALUES` rows, 107 hex constants and 7 live sets, and 278 B-tree indexes with no
hash fallback. At qti-pie HEAD `ace0abd1`, `grizzle generate` reports no changes, `grizzle check` prints
`Qti: ok (1 migration, snapshot 61c625ec…)`, and the import's full write program compiles, 152 inserts and none refused
(lane R87). Then qti-pie moved to `27c4cd2` (`27c4cd20ec4db4171b1743dd275420fb9f7164d6`), its repin `984453d7`, which
adds `str.bytes(n, pattern)`: text of the pattern's language of at most n UTF-8 bytes, a refinement as a pattern is and
not a type, so it meets plain and pattern text in containments, a key reads n exactly, and `keyTextBytes` feeds n
directly. It also refuses a delete that names two facts at one key. Two exports packed identical tarballs, all three
changed, and the skill is unchanged. At the pin, before `keyText`'s flip to bytes, the write program still compiles, 152
inserts and none refused. Then qti-pie moved to `dec8ab3` (`dec8ab32289d91d0190c676e6232ca89a74697a2`), its repin
`89f1b5e9`, where startup decides the keys beyond the primary once (LP-1, below). Two exports packed identical tarballs;
the grizzle and engine tarballs changed, and the testing tarball and the skill did not. The single `_init`, regenerated
from zero, moved the fingerprint from `19ab0214` to `559188a3` with byte-identical SQL: `dec8ab3` keys a refined
language by the IR's `languageKey`, which re-keys ten byte-bounded fields' type hashes, and `grizzle check` gives
`Qti: ok (1 migration, snapshot 559188a3…)`. `dec8ab3` also adds a law kind, `{kind: "generated", relation, field}`,
which an outcome names when a generated column is NULL at a row. Lane S-ORACLE gave each of Qti's four generated fields
a row under an existing law, two under E1 derived for `Operand`'s bits, E1 derived for `SectionSelection.select` and
Calendar date for `StatisticDate.date`, and the census in `refusal.test.ts` requires one per generated field
(`42f4f5d0`). Then qti-pie moved to `1bf3dbb` (`1bf3dbb0a625949c086c28785f60b275d758b2b1`), its repin `dadbbbd4`, which
fixes the `Monoid.or` end (below). Only the engine tarball changed; the grizzle and testing tarballs and the skill are
`dec8ab3`'s. The fingerprint did not move (`559188a3`), so `migrations/qti` is unchanged. Typecheck exits 0, `own` is
OK, R87's `compile.ts` gives 152 inserts and none refused in 11 s, and `grizzle check` gives
`Qti: ok (1 migration, snapshot 559188a3…)`.

### What the pin resolves for qti-pie

- **A set literal as a set operation's operand** (W5's `Bits.of` row, fixed in `80f77bd` and `bf1f470`). PN's
  `ForeignKindSelf` diagonal is gone: "Strictly held" states each foreign kind by
  `Compute.disjoint(StrictlyHeld.row(version, parentKind), Bits.of(NodeKind, [kind]))` (`src/qti/chardata.ts`). A
  literal is never the first operand ("Queries"), so the singleton's subset test is written as the row's disjointness
  from it.
- **Text terms** (`Compute.translate` and `lowerAscii` in `80f77bd` and `bf1f470`, `collapse` and
  `replaceWhitespace` in `e25c2f5`). The three exact comparisons that stood in for them are gone: `lom-title` compares
  titles in `xs:normalizedString`'s value space and tags without regard to case (`src/qti/metadata.ts`), R26
  `document-lang` compares a collapsed `xml:lang` with each listed language without regard to case, and an SSML name
  is compared by its collapsed value (`src/qti/identity.ts`).
- **Hex packs** (`5243c74`). `ChildGrammar`, `Follow`, `Last`, `Symbol`, `GoverningOf` and `AttributeGrammar`
  declared `pack: "hex"` (`src/grammar/install.ts`). At `a2ff5eb` hex is grizzle's one form and the option is gone, so
  the arguments are deleted.
- **Every W5 row fixed in grizzle** above (the axis B-tree rule, closed rows as a set, G1, G2, `UnstatedKey`, the
  query-bounded regex, `GeneratedRefused`) is at the pin, and the four fixes that were uncommitted at `46b2fc3`
  (`powerBounds`, the pure set's put, the span producers' `certified`, the rename's constraint) landed in `99b6217`.

### Defect: a tree insert typed by a recursive member type is refused (TS2615, fixed in `64f0427`)

- **Found by:** PQ's repin, `src/import/write.ts:1149`–`1154` (`Node` and `Instance`) and `src/qti/node.test.ts:251`.
- **Cause:** `NestedUnfit` (`packages/engine/src/types/changeset/changeset.ts:148`–`155` at `99b6217`, reached from
  `MemberCheck` at `:86` through `FitsAnArm` at `:134`), added in `46b2fc3` to refuse a nested member's excess keys,
  maps every nested array with no stopping point. A tree's member relation is its own owner, so a member type of
  unknown depth, `children: readonly Member[]`, which any tree built by a program needs, maps the same pair again
  without end. tsrs reports TS2615 ("Type of property 'children' circularly references itself in mapped type"), and
  `MemberCheck` yields `Refusal<"a member fact is not a fact of the relation">`. `NestedWrites`, in the same file,
  stops at a tree with its `Seen` parameter; `NestedUnfit` has none. The README promises a nested member at any depth ("A nested member,
  at any depth, states no other field", "Families").
- **Reproduction** (nothing of qti-pie; the 99b6217 tarballs only):

  ```ts
  export const Node = relation(
    "Node",
    {
      at: drawn(natural),
      children: drawn(natural),
      doc: uuid,
      end: drawn(natural),
      id: uuid,
      label: pg.integer,
      parent: uuid,
      start: drawn(natural),
    },
    ["id"],
  );
  export const S = schema("Repro", { Node }, [
    tree(on(Node, "parent", "at"), fin(Node, "children"), { root: "doc" }),
  ]);
  type Member = Omit<Addition<typeof Node>, "doc" | "parent"> & {
    readonly children: readonly Member[];
  };
  type Root = Member & { readonly doc: Uuid };
  declare const roots: readonly Root[];
  export const recursive = ChangeSet.empty(S).insert(Node, [...roots]); // TS2615, then TS2345
  ```

  `tsrs --noEmit` (strict) prints `error TS2615: Type of property 'children' circularly references itself in mapped
  type …` twice and `error TS2345: Argument of type 'Root[]' is not assignable to parameter of type 'readonly Root[] &
  Refusal<"a member fact is not a fact of the relation">'`. **Controls:** the same rows as a literal of fixed depth
  typecheck at `99b6217`; the same file typechecks at `4016b69`; a member type written as a plain interface, with no
  `Addition` or `Omit`, fails the same way; and `AdditionIn` is not public, so a caller cannot type its members by
  grizzle's own type.

- **Status:** confirmed by the orchestrator against both tarball sets and the source, and routed to grizzle's beta.1
  work, which reworks the type layer; not filed. qti-pie took no workaround: the three errors stayed red under tsrs at
  `99b6217`.
- **Fixed in `64f0427`:** `NestedUnfit` and `NestedWrites` carry the pairs met above them through one identity test, so
  a recursive member type is checked once per pair (its commit; issue #9's role fix shares it). The reproduction and
  its interface variant typecheck with exit 0 at `64f0427`, and qti-pie's `pnpm check` is green there.

### Small defect: a text cast's refusal names a numeric guard (open at `64f0427`)

- **What happens:** `Compute.cast(x, T)` into a refined text `T` that `x`'s language does not lie inside is
  `PartialOperation` with the precondition `contract`, and its message says `guard it: Compute.when(Compute.within(x,
  <low>, <high>), …)` (`packages/engine/src/scalar/compute/lower.ts:891`–`895` at `99b6217`, which gives every
  `contract` precondition `Compute.within`'s text). `Compute.within` bounds a number; a text's guard is
  `Compute.when(Compute.matches(x, <pattern>), …)` ("Queries").
- **Reproduction:** `relation.generated("Obj", { lang: keyText, node: uuid }, ["node"], (o) => ({ collapsed:
  generated(keyText, Compute.cast(Compute.collapse(o.lang), keyText)) }))` with `keyText =
  str.regex("^.{0,255}.{0,255}.{0,138}$", "su")`, then `grizzle generate`: `PartialOperation at generated field
  Obj.collapsed … guard it: Compute.when(Compute.within(x, <low>, <high>), (x) => Compute.cast(x, text ~
  /^.{0,255}.{0,255}.{0,138}$/su), <your value>)`.
- **The refusal itself is a documented limit:** collapse's image of a pattern other than a freely repeated class or one
  bounded run of at most 257 characters is unbounded ("Queries"), so no bounded text type holds it. qti-pie states
  each collapse in a check instead: the host writes the collapsed value, and `E1_derived` proves `value =
  collapse(lexical)` (`SsmlAttribute`, `GeneralLang`, `PlacedGeneralLang`, `AssessmentObject.langTag`). Not filed.

### The empty-language admission check

- **W5's open row** (`scalar/regex/admit.ts:121` at `46b2fc3`) was unchanged at `99b6217`, and `64f0427` fixes it by
  its commit: the regex IR carries each node's path profiles, so `matchPattern` and `analyzeText` refuse exactly the
  empty languages. Not re-run here: qti-pie states no pattern with a mid-pattern anchor.

### Newly refused at `f66c05b`: the predecessor axis's UNIQUE over `charsAfter`, 8 bytes over

- **What happens:** at `f66c05b`, the schema is refused at compile with `UnrepresentableKey`: the UNIQUE of the tree's
  predecessor axis on `Node` can reach 2,712 bytes, above PostgreSQL's 2,704 (fields `parent` 16 bytes, `at` 8,
  `charsAfter` 2,596, `elements` 8, `end` 8, `state` 2, and the two afters 8 each).
- **Cause:** `keyText`'s bound of 648 code points (`src/qti/limits.ts`), so `charsAfter` is 648 × 4 + 4 = 2,596 bytes.
  The bound was qti-pie's own arithmetic, a re-spelling of grizzle's rule, and at `f66c05b` an index entry counts
  "exact offsets and the null bitmap" (the commit's message), which that arithmetic did not.
- **Classification:** our misuse, a re-spelled bound. The fix is to hold `keyText` to grizzle's public
  `keyTextBytes(fields)`, which lane S-ORACLE's interim bound in `src/qti/limits.ts` does (`e0fcddbf`, "bound keyText by
  grizzle's own keyTextBytes, 646 code points"). `keyTextBytes` alone still leaves this axis 8 bytes over, by the null
  bitmap of its NULL-able hidden columns (the next entry), so the tree holds `keyTextCodePoints` at 646. At grizzle's
  fix, `fce1088`, 646 is exactly what `keyTextBytes(widestTextKey)`, 2,587 bytes, leaves room for. The octet contract
  was to follow at grizzle `a2ff5eb`: there the bound would be N = 2,587 bytes, the largest for which
  `keyTextBytes([...widestTextKey, keyText])` still leaves a byte, with no `proves` needed, because `a2ff5eb`'s bound
  analysis reads `octetLength` off the predicate. A containment cannot copy a text into a contract field, though (the
  entry after next), so the contract is set aside. Instead `keyText` becomes a `str.bytes` refinement bounded by
  `keyTextBytes`, which grizzle added in `27c4cd2` as `str.bytes(n, pattern)` (repin `984453d7`). Lane S-ORACLE made
  that flip in `96df0183`, with no sweep: `src/qti/limits.ts` and its users' bound declarations (`chardata.ts`,
  `attributes.ts` and `node.ts`'s prefix), the oracle's one measure (`utf8Length`), and one `_init` regenerated from
  zero. `keyText` is `str.bytes(keyTextBytes(widestTextKey), any text)`, 2,587 bytes, and every other bounded name is
  bounded likewise. The 646-code-point pattern is gone. `grizzle check` gives
  `Qti: ok (1 migration, snapshot 2c825ffc…)`.

### `keyTextBytes` leaves out a tree axis's null bitmap (grizzle defect, open at `f66c05b`, fixed in `fce1088`)

- **Seen.** The schema refused at compile (`schemaArtifacts`, R87's `work/rv/r87/compile.ts`) with `UnrepresentableKey
  at fold Node at Node.fold.count (src/qti/node.ts:537:20): the UNIQUE of Node the predecessor axis of the tree of Node
  references … its B-tree entry can reach 2712 bytes, above PostgreSQL's 2704-byte limit (field parent 16 bytes, at 8,
  charsAfter 2592, elements 8, end 8, state 2, after Node.children 0 0 8, after Node.children 1 0 8)`. That was with
  `keyText` a pattern of 647 code points. For the same fields, `keyTextBytes([uuid, natural ×5, closedId(Position),
  that 647-code-point pattern])` returns 0, which says a text fits beside them.
- **Cause, from the source at f66c05b.** `textBytesBeside` (engine `schema/representability.ts`, "whatever the key's
  field order and with none of them NULL") lays the datums out from `indexDataOffset(false)`, which is 8. The index
  check `btreeEntryBytes` starts at `indexDataOffset(columns.some((c) => c.nullable))`, which is 16 once any column is
  NULL-able. The tree's predecessor axis carries NULL-able hidden columns: the first child has no predecessor. So its
  entry is 8 bytes past what `keyTextBytes` measured. The public doc of `keyTextBytes` (`public/text.ts`) states no NULL
  caveat, and an author can't declare a NULL-able field, so nothing in the fields passed shows the axis's bitmap.
- **Workaround in this repo.** `src/qti/limits.ts` `keyTextCodePoints` is 646 (`e0fcddbf`), the most for which
  `keyTextBytes` still leaves a byte. The further text it sizes costs 7 bytes (header and worst padding), and the bitmap
  costs 8, so the key text fits exactly where a byte is left. `limits.test.ts` holds 646 to leave a byte and 647 to
  leave none. With grizzle's fix, `fce1088`, the bound is exactly what `keyTextBytes` leaves, measured over a key's
  other fields as its contract reads: `keyTextBytes(widestTextKey)` is 2,587 bytes, so 646 code points fit (2,584
  bytes) and 647 do not, and `keyTextBytes([uuid, OpenLocal])` is 1,638 bytes, so `namespaceText`'s 409 fits and 410
  does not (lane N2, at the repin `8439df91`). 646 and 409 are exact, so the interim is the bound.
- **Classification:** a grizzle defect, confirmed by the grizzle session and fixed before beta.1, in `fce1088`; nothing
  to file. The grizzle session took it in its lane K: `keyTextBytes(fields)` will promise the worst case over every
  index grizzle plans over those fields, the null bitmap and hidden columns included, under a generated test that holds
  the bound admitted and tight. qti-pie's 646 interim was `e0fcddbf`, and `96df0183` replaced it with the byte bound
  itself, 2,587 bytes, so no code-point bound remains.
- **Fixed in `fce1088` (`fce10882b08165ebb44125fc0c9ddac6572c6495`),** pushed to grizzle `main` with its gate green, and
  checked at `fce1088`, which `git ls-remote` showed as `origin/main` on 2026-11-06: "keyTextBytes leaves room for an
  index tuple's null bitmap, so a text at its result fits every index planned over its fields, a key over a selection
  and a family's axis included" (its message). qti-pie repinned to it in `8439df91` (lane N2), and N2 measured the edge
  there: the bound is exactly what `keyTextBytes` leaves, so 646 and 409 stand, and reading 133's numbers with them.

### A text contract can't be the target of a copy (a limit; closed by `str.bytes(n, pattern)` in grizzle `27c4cd2`)

- **Seen.** keyText as the ruled octet contract, `contract("KeyText", str.regex("^.*$", "su"), (v) =>
  Compute.octetLength(v).le(N))`, typechecked in a scratch copy of pantry. The 54 containments that copy a document's
  text into a keyText field were refused with `Refusal<"the sides' field types differ">`, e.g. `declarations.ts:537`,
  `contained(on(identifiers, "node", "lexical"), on(Declaration, "node", "identifierLexical"))`: `Attribute.lexical` is
  a text, `Lexical`, and the target is `Contract<"KeyText", …>`. The same containments type-check while keyText is a
  pattern, since a pattern is no part of a text's type (`src/qti/limits.ts`).
- **Why no workaround.** A containment's side is a list of fields, so no `Compute.base` can read through it. The
  source can't be keyText either, since `Attribute.lexical` is unbounded by design. Branding at the host (`keyText(v)`)
  fixes only the 460 host-side type errors, not these.
- **Standalone reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/contract-containment/` (`repro.ts` and its
  `tsc.out`): a containment from a plain text field into a field of a text contract is refused, and the control into a
  pattern-bounded field (`^.{0,100}$`) passes. At run time `own(schemaDescOf(S))` succeeds (`run.out`), so the refusal
  is grizzle's types alone, and its runtime admission disagrees with them; grizzle should settle which one is meant.
- **Classification:** a limit at `f66c05b` and `a2ff5eb`; the feature is accepted for grizzle beta.1 as `str.bytes(N)`.
  Containment's one-type rule is grizzle's documented typing, so this is no defect. The grizzle session accepted the
  ask in its lane K, after the `keyTextBytes` fix: a byte bound as a text refinement, `str.bytes(N)`, with the CHECK
  `octet_length(VALUE) <= N`, read exactly by the bound analysis. Like a pattern, it is no part of the type, so it meets
  plain and pattern-refined text in containments, and N comes from `keyTextBytes(fields)`. The reproduction above
  becomes one of grizzle's kept cases. Nothing to file.
- **Closed in `27c4cd2` (`27c4cd20ec4db4171b1743dd275420fb9f7164d6`):** grizzle added the bound as
  `str.bytes(n, pattern)`, text of the pattern's language of at most n UTF-8 bytes. Its CHECK sits beside the pattern's,
  n is read exactly, it meets plain and pattern text in containments, and `keyTextBytes` feeds n directly. qti-pie
  repinned to it in `984453d7` (lane S-ORACLE) and flipped `keyText` to it in `96df0183`: `keyText` is
  `str.bytes(keyTextBytes(widestTextKey), any text)`, 2,587 bytes, and every other bounded name is bounded likewise. The
  workaround, the 646-code-point pattern of `e0fcddbf`, is gone.

### A containment's reverse index refuses a source column of no byte bound (defect, fixed in `4b155e8`)

At grizzle `f66c05b` and every pin after it, `a2ff5eb` and `fce1088` among them, the Qti schema does not compile. The
first refusal comes from the reverse B-tree index that grizzle plans over a containment's source columns:

```
grizzle: refused at stage generate (schema Qti): the schema does not compile: UnrepresentableKey at contained
Attribute(node, name, lexical, document, kind) selected in Reference(node, name, lexical, document, kind) at
Attribute.contained (…/src/qti/package.ts:699:3): the index of Attribute over the columns the containment … references
from: field lexical …
```

When that law is set aside, the next is `src/qti/metadata.ts:892`, the containment of `stringLanguageAttributes`'s
`lexical` into `LomStringLanguage.language`. Both read `Attribute.lexical`, typed `Lexical = str.regex(charsPattern)`
(`src/qti/attributes.ts:148`, field at `:213`). That is an attribute's lexical form as written, which no pattern
bounds. The target columns are bounded: `Reference.lexical` is `keyText`. So the containment's foreign key and the
target's UNIQUE are both representable, and only the source's index is refused.

- **Checked at:** grizzle `fce1088` (`fce10882b08165ebb44125fc0c9ddac6572c6495`, the pin at qti-pie HEAD `018fe65b`), in
  the clone at `~/Documents/grizzle`. `fce1088` is its newest `main`, at 17:11 on 2026-11-06.
- **Cause:** `#scalarContainment`, `packages/engine/src/laws/model/plan.ts:1027-1074` at `fce1088`.
  - It plans the containment's foreign key and the target's UNIQUE (`#targetUnique`, `:1081-1100`).
  - Then it plans a `ReverseIndex` over every source column (`sourceColumns`, `:1063-1072`), whose entry it proves by
    `plannedEntry` (`:184-203`).
  - `plannedEntry` refuses a column whose datum has no byte bound with `UnrepresentableKey { kind: "unboundedText" }`
    (`:196-198`; the sentence is `core/causes.ts:338`).
  - The PERIOD coverage's reverse index takes the same entry and the same refusal (`plan.ts:1229-1238`).
  - The entry check on the reverse index is new at `f66c05b`. At `64f0427` the index carried no `plannedEntry`
    (`git show 64f0427:…/plan.ts` has no `references from` entry), and the reproduction's case generates there.
- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/reverse-index/`. It needs nothing of qti-pie: `pack/`
  holds the tarballs packed from `fce1088`, byte-identical to qti-pie's `vendor/` (sha256 `b0be260a…` and
  `61a5da98…`). `run.sh` runs `grizzle generate` and `grizzle check` on three schemas. Its output is in `run.out`,
  verbatim:
  - **case:** `Attr { id: uuid, value: str.regex("^[a-z]*$") }` and `Ref { id: uuid, value: str.regex("^[a-z]{0,64}$")
    }`, with `contained(on(Attr, "id", "value"), on(Ref, "id", "value"))`. It is refused at generate and at check:
    `UnrepresentableKey at contained Attr(id, value) in Ref(id, value) at Attr.contained (…/case.ts:9:50): the index of
    Attr over the columns the containment of Attr(id, value) in Ref(id, value) references from: field value is text
    whose pattern proves no byte bound; a bounded anchored pattern, or a contract bounding its charLength or
    octetLength, does` (exit 1 each).
  - **control:** the same containment with `Attr.value` bounded as `Ref.value` is, `str.regex("^[a-z]{0,64}$")`. It
    generates, and check prints `Repro: ok (1 migration, snapshot d25ded70…)` (exit 0).
  - **bounded-prefix control:** the unbounded column with the containment over `id` alone. It generates and checks
    (exit 0).
  - The same case at `64f0427` generates (exit 0).
- **Classification:** a grizzle defect, an overreach of the D3 fix. The ruling, from the grizzle session (grizzle-c5)
  in its reply to the Mind at 17:15 on 2026-11-06:
  - an index's width never refuses a containment;
  - the reverse B-tree goes over the longest leading run of bounded source columns that fits one entry;
  - with no such run, the index is a hash index on one column;
  - PERIOD coverage gets the same rule, and the GiST bound is fixed;
  - only the target's UNIQUE can refuse.
- **Status:** fixed in `4b155e8` (`4b155e8a88860a5cac544f38fe664f33d43a0a98`), grizzle's lane K, pushed to grizzle
  `main` with its gate green before beta.1, so nothing is filed. `git ls-remote` showed it as grizzle `origin/main` on
  2026-11-06. By grizzle-c5's report of the fix:
  - the reverse B-tree goes over the longest leading run of referencing columns whose entry fits, else it is a hash
    index on one column;
  - PERIOD coverage follows the same rule, with the GiST page as its bound;
  - only the referenced UNIQUE must be bounded.

  The commit's message is a copy of `fce1088`'s and does not describe this change, so it is not quoted here; grizzle
  was asked to correct the record going forward.

  Lane R87 packed tarballs from a scratch export of `4b155e8` (sha256 `c47013f1…` for grizzle, `92d16745…` for the
  engine) and re-ran the reproduction, its output verbatim in `run-4b155e8.out`:
  - **case:** `grizzle generate` exits 0, and `grizzle check` prints `Repro: ok (1 migration, snapshot 2c4ee745…)` and
    exits 0. The draft has the foreign key from `Attr(id, value)` to `Ref(id, value)` and `Ref`'s UNIQUE `(id, value)`,
    and no index over the unbounded `Attr.value`;
  - **control** (bounded source): `Repro: ok (1 migration, snapshot d25ded70…)`, exit 0, the same snapshot as at
    `fce1088`;
  - **bounded-prefix control:** `Repro: ok (1 migration, snapshot 2071c499…)`, exit 0, also unchanged.

  So the case generates and checks at `4b155e8`, and both controls stay ok. qti-pie took the fix at lane N2's repin
  B, `a60eb2c1`. At qti-pie HEAD `ace0abd1` the `Qti` schema compiles: `grizzle generate` reports no changes, and
  `grizzle check` prints `Qti: ok (1 migration, snapshot 61c625ec…)` (lane R87).

- **qti-pie:** no workaround. `Attribute.lexical` stays unbounded, as an attribute's lexical form is. Until the fix was
  pinned, at `a60eb2c1`:
  - no snapshot can be generated at HEAD and no layer builds;
  - the workload guard's two free tests (`src/qti/logins.test.ts` and `src/test/gen/assembly.compile.test.ts`,
    `bbe5f4ca` and `d11c39f1`) are red, with `schema Qti is not the one snapshot.json records`.

### A counted family's `Monoid.or` end is re-read through the wrong text form (defect, fixed in `1bf3dbb`)

- **Triage:** lane N2, at grizzle `27c4cd2`. The five files below are byte-identical at `4b155e8` and `30af956`.
- **Checked at:** grizzle `30af956`, its newest `main` when N2 ran the pull-first check at 19:05 on 2026-11-06, from
  tarballs packed from a `git archive` of it (grizzle `f10a6013…`, engine `c7d281ac…`). It still fails there:
  `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/fold-closed-row-30af956/run-30af956.out` has the case and the host-bits variant
  `invariant-rejected` with the same DETAIL, and the sum control `accepted`. grizzle `main` has since moved to
  `4ec7574`, and the reproduction was not re-run there.
- **Cause:**
  - `bind.ts`'s `carried` refills the owner's fact from `recountValues` (`packages/engine/src/write/program/bind.ts:427`
    to `484`, the call at `:452`).
  - The recount's ends are parameter texts: `textOf` is `admittedText` (`packages/engine/src/write/family/edit.ts:135`
    to `136`, the ends at `:916`).
  - `recountValues` reads them back with `scalarCodec(…).convert` (`edit.ts:194` to `209`, the read at `:203`), which
    reads `bit_out` text (`packages/engine/src/codec/scalar.ts:52`). `convertBits` collects the positions of `"1"`
    (`packages/engine/src/scalar/values/convert.ts:60` to `68`).
  - So the hex parameter text `"XC"`, which is {a, b}, reads back as ∅. N2's trace of the host-bits run shows it
    (`~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/fold-closed-row-27c4cd2/trace-27c4cd2.out`, captured at 19:07 on 2026-11-06):
    `N2TRACE recount ends bases: host=Set{a,b} text="XC"`. The owner's INSERT then binds
    `[…"{\"X0\"}","{\"2\"}"]`, bases `X0`, the empty set, beside members 2, while the members' after is
    `{"X8","XC"}`, so the owner's end matches no member's after.
  - It is a regression from `a2ff5eb`, where hex became the one form. At `f66c05b`, `codec/scalar.ts:51`'s parameter
    text was binary, the same as `bit_out`. A count or sum end is unaffected.
- **Root cause, confirmed by grizzle-c5 in its own tree:**
  - **Made at** `edit.ts:916`, where `planEdits`' recount builds its ends with `textOf` = `admittedText`, the parameter
    text: `"X"` and hex, so {a, b} over [a, b, c] is `"XC"`.
  - **Misread at** `edit.ts:203`, where `recountValues` reads that text with `scalarCodec(…).convert`, the reader of
    `bit_out`'s binary text (`convert.ts:60` to `68`). `"XC"` holds no `'1'`, so it reads as ∅, and a hex digit `1`
    sets the handle at that character's index. That is the CLI tier's "live handles … 23514".
  - **Reached from** `bind.ts:452` (the owner's insert refilled from the recount), `:499` (a nested owner) and `:556`
    (other families' views).
  - **Scope:** since `a2ff5eb`, bits is the only carrier whose parameter text and output text differ. So only
    `Monoid.or` and `twos` ends over bits come out wrong, and only on the recount path: an owner inserted in the same
    ChangeSet, or an owner with two families.
- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/fold-closed-row/` (`run-4b155e8.out`), and the same at `27c4cd2` in
  `fold-closed-row-27c4cd2/run-27c4cd2.out`. A counted family with a `Monoid.or` end over a closed set:
  - **case:** `invariant-rejected`, with nothing written. The server log holds `insert or update on table "owner"
    violates foreign key constraint "__owner_fk_id_pred_members_bases_qxyoofnu37"`, with `DETAIL: Key (id,
    __pred_members_vgfnsqbvv6, bases)=(00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001, 1, 0000) is not present in table
    "member"`: the owner's end, `0000`, is the empty set;
  - **host-bits variant:** the same fold with the bits written by the host, also `invariant-rejected`, so the closed
    row is not the cause;
  - **sum control:** the same family with a `Monoid.sum` end, `accepted`, its total `12` over `2` postings.
- **Effect in qti-pie:** `Expression`'s five `Monoid.or` ends over the counted `Operand` family arrive empty, so
  `E1_derived` refuses the every-relation package's `qti-sum`. The CLI tier's "writes only live handles … bit(112) …
  a family's insert of Node raised 23514" is the same defect, as grizzle-c5 confirmed.
- **Status:** relayed to the grizzle session (grizzle-c5) under the operator's order that it be fixed with a
  search-based guard that keeps it from recurring. grizzle-c5 confirmed the defect, and ruled at 19:25 on 2026-11-06
  that the repair is by representation, as its lane FOLD's cutover:
  - host values only, through the fill and the recount, which never re-parses a text it wrote;
  - parameter text and output text become distinct branded types, so feeding one to the other's reader cannot
    compile;
  - then an audit that deletes every place grizzle reads back its own text, then the guard.

  The guard is a round trip over every carrier through PostgreSQL and the fold-end search over monoids, carriers,
  family kinds and write forms, with planted bugs, `edit.ts:203` among them, restored and shown caught. It is being
  fixed before beta.1, so nothing is filed. qti-pie takes no workaround.

- **Fixed in grizzle `1bf3dbb`** (`1bf3dbb0a625949c086c28785f60b275d758b2b1`), pushed to grizzle `main` with its full
  gate green; `git ls-remote` shows it as `origin/main`.
  - **The fix:** the recount carries host values end to end, with one encode at `planRelation` and `planOwners`, and
    nothing reads back a text grizzle wrote.
  - **A second defect of the same class,** found by the new round trip: the codec test's wire sizing used the roster's
    size, not its hex width.
  - **The guard:** an independent fold model over every monoid and plane; bits widths 1 to 9; counted, ordered and
    tree families; every write form; 0 to 4 members exhausted; on the unit tier and on PostgreSQL, with every cell
    required and this case kept.
  - **Planted bugs, each caught:** an identity end, a first-member end, a dropped high hex digit, and the old
    `edit.ts:203` read.
  - **Still to come:** lane FOLD's part 2, distinct branded types for parameter text and output text.
  - **Confirmed downstream by lane N2** at `1bf3dbb`, from tarballs packed from a `git archive` of it (grizzle
    `80a2dcaf…`, engine `a4cd1c90…`): in `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/fold-closed-row-1bf3dbb/run-1bf3dbb.out` the case,
    the host-bits variant and the sum control are all `accepted`, and the owner's end is `bases` `"1100"`, which is
    {a, b}, beside `members` `"2"`.
  - **qti-pie:** repinned to `1bf3dbb` in `dadbbbd4` (repin E), with the fingerprint unchanged (`559188a3`) and
    `grizzle check` clean. Nothing is filed.

### A CHECK's refusal drops PostgreSQL's DETAIL row (a reporting limit, accepted by grizzle)

When a write breaks a CHECK, grizzle's refusal says "the database names no row", though PostgreSQL's error carries a
DETAIL naming the failing row (as the server log above shows for a foreign key). This is a reporting limit, not a
wrong verdict. Asked as a question, grizzle accepted it: a refused CHECK will carry the failing row's key from
PostgreSQL's DETAIL, in its lane OPEN as "witness a CHECK refusal on the host". Nothing is filed.

### A law's key that closes a cycle with a family's owner axis is installed immediate, and no write can order it (grizzle defect, fixed in `e9227af`; lane DBT)

- **Checked at:** grizzle `27c4cd2` and trunk `4ec7574`; both files are unchanged between them.
- **Cause:**
  - README "Direct SQL access" 23503 (`README.md:2441` at `27c4cd2`) defers a key that "lies on a cycle of foreign
    keys".
  - `#deferral` (`packages/engine/src/laws/model/model.ts:1053-1065`) builds that cycle from the laws' own keys
    (`deferral === "rule"`) and period keys only, and leaves out owner axes, which are immediate (`:1526`).
  - So `select(O, n=1)(node, language) → M(node, language)`, whose target `M` holds no other field, is immediate beside
    `M`'s owner axis `M(node, copy) → O(node, n)`.
  - The scheduler (`packages/engine/src/write/frontend/schedule.ts:48-51`, `:67-72`) drops one edge of the cycle, so
    the write compiles, and then PostgreSQL refuses `O`'s INSERT with 23503 whenever both sides hold rows.
- **Seen:** qti-pie's `LomString`→`LomStringLanguage` and `AssessmentObject`→`ObjectLang`, the `4b155e8` `_init`'s
  only two cross-table cycles of immediate FKs. They refused `sharedMedia` and `metaData` in N2's tier at `5ca47304`.
- **Reproduction:**
  - schema: `O {node uuid, language text, n drawn(natural)}` keyed by `node`; `M {node uuid, language text}` keyed by
    `node`;
  - laws: `counted(on(M, 'node'), fin(O, 'n'))`; a check on `O` (`n = 1 or language = ''`);
    `contained(select(O, n = 1)(node, language), M(node, language))`;
  - write: insert `O` and `M` with `node` and `'en'`;
  - expected: invariant-rejected 23503 on the containment's key at `O`'s INSERT, with generate drafting that key
    without DEFERRABLE;
  - control: the containment turned to `M` → `O` is accepted.
- **Workaround in qti-pie (removed in `5eb05874`):** both laws were stated member → owner (`a6d23fcc`), which is
  equivalent under the count and E1, and the guard `src/test/write-order.test.ts` restated the deferral rule over Qti's
  statements.
- **Confirmed by lane N2** at grizzle `27c4cd2` and at its newest `main`, `dec8ab3`
  (`dec8ab32289d91d0190c676e6232ca89a74697a2`), with the same results in
  `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/deferral-owner-cycle/run-27c4cd2.out` and `run-dec8ab3.out`:
  - **case:** generate and check pass (`N2DeferCycle: ok (1 migration, snapshot bfc36e51…)`). The drafted `O`→`M` key
    has no DEFERRABLE, and `M`'s owner axis is immediate. The one-ChangeSet write is `invariant-rejected`, with
    `insert or update on table "o" violates foreign key constraint "__o_fk_node_language_…"` and `DETAIL: Key (…)=(…d1,
    en) is not present in table "m"`;
  - **control:** the containment turned `M` → `O` is accepted (`N2DeferTurned: ok (1 migration, snapshot
    2b51d625…)`).
  - **Anchors:** `model.ts:1050-1051`'s comment, "A family axis fixes its own deferral and is no edge of the cycle
    graph…", is the stated reason owner axes are left out.
- **Status:** handed to grizzle-c5, whose lane F takes it as a defect, with a generated-search guard and this case kept.
  Nothing is filed. The workaround, `a6d23fcc`, was removed in `5eb05874` (below).
- **Fixed in `e9227af`** (`e9227afd2fa183bba52d74829b38aa52e973cef0`), by grizzle's report: a cycle through an owner
  axis is now deferred or refused, and this case is kept in grizzle's `test/pg/write/deferral/owner-cycle.test.ts`. Lane
  N2 confirmed it downstream (below). qti-pie's repin to `e9227af` is held on the `UnorderableStratum` entry below. By
  the operator's ruling that qti-pie carries no workaround, the workaround was removed in `5eb05874`: the laws are owner
  into member again, `contained(select(LomString, languages = 1)(node, language), LomStringLanguage(node, language))`
  and its `AssessmentObject` → `ObjectLang` twin; `src/test/write-order.test.ts` is deleted, since grizzle states the
  rule itself (`e9227af` defers or refuses a cycle through an owner axis); and the `_init` is regenerated,
  `20261107041337_init`, snapshot `b903bdfd`. Until repin F brings `e9227af`, the LOM-language imports, `sharedMedia`
  and `metaData`, are refused with 23503 at `1bf3dbb`.
- **Confirmed downstream by lane N2** at `e9227af`, from tarballs packed from a `git archive` of it (grizzle
  `d1a32154…`, engine `e3978234…`), in `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/deferral-owner-cycle/run-e9227af.out`:
  - **case:** now `accepted`, where at `27c4cd2` and `dec8ab3` it was `invariant-rejected` with 23503 at `O`'s insert.
    Both keys on the cycle now draft `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED`: `O`→`M`'s containment key,
    `__o_fk_node_language_…`, and `M`'s owner-axis copy, `__m_fk_node_copy_n_…`. At `27c4cd2` and `dec8ab3` both were
    immediate;
  - **control:** `accepted`, and its keys stay immediate, as they close no cycle;
  - **snapshots:** `N2DeferCycle` moves to `6af324cb…` (it was `bfc36e51…`), and `N2DeferTurned` is unchanged at
    `2b51d625…`.

### A closed `C.row` read whose key is an absent `C.at` casts NULL to a bits domain, whose CHECK raises 23514 (grizzle defect, confirmed; lane DBT)

- **Checked at:** the vendored `dec8ab3`, and grizzle `main` `0a8f697` for the pull-first check.
- **Cause:**
  - grizzle casts `C.row`'s completions to the set's bits domain (`packages/engine/src/sql/constants.ts:285`).
  - That domain's CHECK is `((VALUE & L()) = VALUE) IS TRUE` (`packages/engine/src/physical/ddl/constants.ts:1002`
    to `1010`).
  - PostgreSQL runs a domain CHECK on NULL (`execExpr.c` `ExecInitCoerceToDomain`; `execExprInterp.c:4498-4508`), so
    the cast raises before the law's three-valued OR can hold by its other disjunct.
  - That contradicts grizzle's own statement that an absent key reads "the empty set for C.row"
    (`packages/engine/src/physical/ddl/expr.ts:242-244`; `README.md:1427-1428`).
- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/grizzle-trunk/packages/grizzle/work/null-set-read/` (`run.sql` over the drafted
  migration, in one SERIALIZABLE transaction rolled back), generated and checked on grizzle `main` `0a8f697`. Its
  output, `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/null-set-read.out`:
  - **scalar control:** the scalar read, absent at kind b, holds by `kind <> a`: `INSERT 0 1`;
  - **held-key control:** the set read where the key is held, kind a carrying {x}: `INSERT 0 1`;
  - **the case:** the set read, absent at kind b, which should hold as the scalar one does, is refused:
    `ERROR:  value for domain null_set_read.__flag_bits_4_urrfzqnzt6 violates check constraint "check"`;
  - **why:** a bare `SELECT NULL::bit varying::"null_set_read"."__flag_bits_4_urrfzqnzt6"` is refused with the same
    error.
- **Effect in qti-pie:** `Node`'s `picture_source_srcset` and `track_srclang` CHECKs read
  `SlotSelf.row(AttributeSlotOf.at("slot", model, name))`, whose key is absent on all but 2 to 4 of 231 models. So
  every lawful `Node` row is refused: node.db's six direct-SQL cases, and every package import. The pin evidence is in
  `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/t2/logs/node-pin.out` and `node-pin-2.out`.
- **Status:** relayed to grizzle-c5 under the operator's standing order: fixed at the root, with a search guard over
  closed reads with absent keys in every position, planted bugs, and this case kept. Nothing is filed and no
  workaround is applied. DBT's `Compute.cond` guard is recorded only as an option not taken.

### `AriaRange`'s immediate key into `AriaValue` is refused at import (grizzle defect, fixed upstream in `e9227af`; lane AR)

- **Checked at:** the vendored `1bf3dbb` and grizzle `e9227af` (`e9227afd2fa183bba52d74829b38aa52e973cef0`),
  `origin/main` when fetched on 2026-11-06.
- **The law** (`src/qti/interactions.ts`, the containments after `ariaValueFamily`): `AriaRange(node, max)` is
  contained in `AriaValue(node, value)` where `name = aria-valuemax`, and the same for `min`. `AriaValue` is a member
  of the counted family `ariaValueFamily`, owned by `AriaElement`.
- **The rows are right.** `readProgram` on `everyRelationPackage({choices: 2, hotspots: 1, hottexts: 1})` builds
  `AriaValue` `{name: "aria-valuemax", value: "10"}` and `{name: "aria-valuemin", value: "0"}`, and `AriaRange`
  `{max: "10", min: "0"}`, for the same node. Both are text, collapsed and equal.
- **The cause, at `1bf3dbb`:**
  - The installed key `__aria_range_fk_node_max_tag_name_aria_valuemax_ptxqw5jpmd`
    (`migrations/qti/20261107020714_init/02-structure.sql:5826`, and `:5828` for `min`) has no DEFERRABLE.
  - The deferral rule makes it immediate: the target's fields are all referenced, and the cycle search leaves owner
    axes out (engine `laws/model/model.ts:1079-1094`, "a family's members are written only through its planner,
    which orders them"). This is the same cause as lane DBT's owner-axis deferral entry above.
  - The flat insert of a counted member becomes a family edit, not an action (`write/frontend/compile.ts:1462-1480`),
    so the stratum's scheduler never orders it against `AriaRange` (`compile.ts:1713-1733`, `schedule.ts`
    `scheduleStratum`). The family statements are placed "after every statement of its stratum"
    (`write/program/compile.ts:695-703`).
  - In the compiled import program, statement 0 (the plain stratum, one statement) inserts `aria_element` and
    `aria_range`, and statement 192 inserts `aria_value`. The immediate key is checked at the end of statement 0,
    and refused with 23503.
- **At `e9227af`:** `AriaRange` → `AriaValue` and the owner axis are deferred, because the owner axis counts in the
  cycle search and `AriaElement` → `AriaRange` → `AriaValue` → `AriaElement` is a cycle. The acyclic form is ordered by
  the joint schedule, so both forms are fixed there. Standalone reproduction:
  `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/ar-order/` (case, cycle, control), and lane N2's SQL run of it,
  `~/.cache/gr8des/n2/repro/ar-order-n2.out`, where the immediate key refuses `r`'s insert with `Key (node, v)=(…0001,
  10) is not present in table "m"`.
- **Classification:** confirmed, a grizzle defect, fixed in `e9227af`.
- **qti-pie:** no workaround. The import needs the repin, which the `UnorderableStratum` entry below blocks.

### `InteractionAttribute`'s immediate key into `SectionTemplateName` refuses a fragment's template reference at import (the owner-axis ordering defect, fixed upstream in `e9227af`; lane OL)

- **Seen:** `package.packaging`'s `rtest01-template-reference` (N2's log `src_import_package.packaging.test.ts.log:527`).
  It adds `<qti-template-inline template-identifier="NONE">` to `rtest01`'s shared fragment, which the oracle rightly
  refuses under `template-ref`. The database reports a `LocateDisagreement` instead, under `contained
  InteractionAttribute(node, name, kind, document, value) in SectionTemplateName(…)`.
- **The rows are right:** `readProgram` writes one `InteractionAttribute` `{template-identifier, qti-template-inline,
  document rtest01-fragment.xml, value NONE}` and the matching `SectionTemplateName` `{identifier "NONE"}`, so the
  containment holds. The schema's source (`fragmentReferenceOf(references.template)`) and the host's
  (`sectionNamesOf(…)`) select the same attributes.
- **Cause:** `SectionTemplateName` is a counted member of `sectionTemplateNameFamily`, owned by `ReferringDocument`.
  The key into it, `__interaction_attribute_fk_node_name_kind_document_5huu3vxx2z`
  (`20261107041337_init/02-structure.sql:6354`), has no DEFERRABLE, and neither has
  `__section_template_ref_fk_node_attribute_kind_…` (`:7068`). A family's members are inserted after their stratum's
  statements, so the immediate key is checked before its row exists: the `AriaRange` class, above.
- **Classification:** a grizzle defect, fixed in `e9227af` by grizzle's report. It is not re-run yet, since that needs
  the repin, which the `UnorderableStratum` entry below blocks. No schema change and no workaround.

### A ChangeSet chain of more than about 94 inserts is TS2589 at `e9227af` (grizzle defect, type layer)

- **Checked at:** grizzle `e9227af`, under grizzle's own checker (effect-tsgo `tsrs`) and under `tsc` 7.0.2. It is a
  regression from `1bf3dbb`.
- **Cause:** a ChangeSet's strata, its sixth type parameter (`packages/engine/src/types/changeset/changeset.ts:466`,
  `:473`), are rebuilt by every insert (`:482-486`) through `WithMember` (`:586`), which nests over the previous
  stratum. So n inserts make n nested instantiations, past the depth limit of 100.
- **Symptom:** `chain100` gives TS2589 seven times, and from the 95th insert on TS2345, "a member fact is not a fact
  of the relation". The controls: `chain100` is ok at `1bf3dbb`, and `chain90` and `union100` are ok at `e9227af`.
- **Files:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/chain-depth/`: the sources `chain100.ts`, `chain90.ts`
  and `union100.ts` with their `tsconfig.json`; `tsrs.out`, grizzle's own `effect-tsgo tsrs` at `e9227af`, TS2589 seven
  times at `chain100.ts(104,22)` and then TS2345; `tsrs-control.out`, `chain90` and `union100` exiting 0;
  `tc-e9227af.out`, TypeScript 7.0.2 at `e9227af` with the same errors; and `tc-1bf3dbb.out`, `chain100` at `1bf3dbb`
  exiting 0.
- **Effect in qti-pie:** `changeSetOf`'s 152 inserts gave 117 errors in `write.ts`, and cascades from them.
- **Workaround:** none: qti-pie stays pinned at `1bf3dbb`, where the 152-insert chain typechecks, until grizzle's
  strata-depth fix. The three-way `ChangeSet.union` split that was planned for repin F is not landing, by the operator's
  ruling that qti-pie carries no workaround, so `changeSetOf` stays one chain.
- **Status:** relayed to grizzle-c5, with a guard asked for: a 300-insert chain that typechecks in linear time, with
  a planted nested variant shown failing. Not filed.

### `UnorderableStratum` on qti-pie's import ChangeSet at `e9227af`, whose cycle is not real (grizzle defect, confirmed; lane AR)

- **What happens:** `e9227af`'s single schedule, in which plain and family statements share one order, refuses qti-pie's
  152-insert stratum:
  `changeSetOf: REFUSED: UnorderableStratum at ChangeSet: the stratum's count of ChoiceVariableName, then count of Document, …`,
  a cycle of 52 statements through the count of `ReferringDocument`, the insert of `SectionScope` and the update of
  `SectionTemplateRef` (R87's compile at `e9227af`,
  `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/chain-depth/clean-f-r87.out`). At `1bf3dbb` the same stratum compiled.
- **The cycle is not real** (lane AR, checked at `e9227af`, still `origin/main`):
  - The NOT DEFERRABLE foreign keys among the cycle's 31 relations number 4 in `e9227af`'s own draft, and they form no
    cycle among relations: `AssembledLang` → `DocumentGenerals`, `AssembledReference` → `TestReference`, `SectionScope`
    → `ReferringDocument`, and `SectionTemplateRef` → `SectionScope` (its owner axis, ON UPDATE CASCADE). The `1bf3dbb`
    draft has 10, among them the owner axes of `Site`, `VariableRef` and `XmlLang` and `DocumentLang`'s key, which
    `e9227af` defers.
  - The 52-statement cycle has 2 key edges and 50 chain edges. The key edges run from the count of `ReferringDocument`
    to the insert of `SectionScope` (its key over the counts), then to the update of `SectionTemplateRef` (the owner
    axis). The chain edges come from `write/frontend/schedule.ts:158-163`, where each family statement follows the
    previous one. That makes one total order over every family of the stratum (deletes, then updates in place, then
    counts, then inserts; `compile.ts:1745-1786`, `familyNodesOf`), so an update of `SectionTemplateRef` precedes the
    count of `ReferringDocument`, a family it has no key to.
  - The evidence: `e9227af`'s scheduler run on the import ChangeSet, patched at load time, with a Tarjan search for
    cyclic components (`~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/ar-order/pantry-schedule/`, `sched.out` and
    `immediate-fks.out`). As shipped, it finds 1 component of 90 statements, and the ChangeSet is refused. With the chain
    kept only between related family statements (the same relation, or an owner and its member) and every key edge
    kept, it finds 0 components, and the ChangeSet compiles. With update and count edges kept only where their columns
    meet the key's and the total chain kept, it is still refused, with 64 statements.
  - The standalone 4-relation reproduction is `ar-order` `unorderable`: it fails at `e9227af` and compiles at
    `1bf3dbb`.
- **Classification:** a grizzle defect, introduced by `e9227af`'s joint schedule, the fix for the `AriaRange` class.
- **Status:** relayed to grizzle-c5's lane F as a hotfix with a guard. Nothing is filed. qti-pie carries no workaround:
  `ChangeSet.sequence`, which the refusal suggests, would split the import into strata, and is ruled out. It blocks
  repin F.

### A nested write's dedupe signature concatenates every member's full content text, so a lawful write past V8's string limit dies in the host (grizzle defect, confirmed; lane N2)

- **Checked at:** `1bf3dbb` and grizzle's newest `main`, `e9227af`, with identical results.
- **Cause:**
  - `nestedRows`' signature is a `textsKey` of every reached row's identity (`write/family/nesting.ts:326-332`);
  - `textsKey` concatenates `${length}:${text}` and throws `RangeError` at line 53 once the string passes V8's
    `MAX_STRING_LENGTH`, 536,870,888 (`write/family/draw.ts:50-56`);
  - the call path is `normalize.ts:498`, then `bind.ts:182`, then `adapter/apply/run.ts:239`, all before any SQL.
- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/texts-key/` (`run-1bf3dbb.out`, `run-e9227af.out`):
  - **case:** 7 members of 80,000,000 characters each, 560,000,000 together: `failure: RangeError: Invalid string
    length`, `at textsKey (@bjornpagen/grizzle-engine/dist/write/family/draw.js:53:30)`, from `nestedRows`
    (`nesting.js:330:21`), `normalizeChangeSet` (`normalize.js:498:20`) and `bindWrite` (`bind.js:182:24`);
  - **control:** 6 members of 80,000,000 characters, 480,000,000 together: `outcome: accepted`.
- **Effect in qti-pie:** `src/import/package.large.db.test.ts`, 13 bulk entries of 64 MiB each, dies in the host.
- **Status:** relayed to grizzle-c5 under the operator's standing order: a root fix (a digest or a structural
  identity), a guard search past the limit, a cost gate, a planted concatenation, and this case kept. Nothing is filed,
  and qti-pie carries no workaround.

## A role lacked EXECUTE on what its CHECKs cast to (grizzle defect, fixed in `f66c05b`)

- **Found by:** seat N1's database tier on server A at `64f0427` (`docs/rfc/swarm-ledger.md`, 14:05 on 2026-11-06).
  `qti__author` lacked EXECUTE on 3 of the 7 roster `_live_` functions, whose bits domains appear only as casts inside
  CHECKs, so PostgreSQL refused every `Node` and `Attribute` insert. `src/qti/attributes.db.test.ts` (5 cases) and
  `src/qti/node.db.test.ts` (1) went red on it.
- **Cause**, at `64f0427`: `roleExecutes` (`packages/engine/src/physical/ddl/roles.ts:1162` to `1180`) grants EXECUTE
  on what `callsOf` and `reached` (`packages/engine/src/physical/ddl/context.ts:413` to `440`) find in the role's
  statements. Those statements are the CHECKs of the domains in `use.domains`, the tables the role writes and its
  views. A domain reached only through a cast inside a CHECK is not in `use.domains`, so its CHECK's calls are never
  reached. The doc comment (`roles.ts:1156` to `1158`) promises the CHECKs "which a write and a cast to the domain run",
  since PostgreSQL checks EXECUTE against the invoker.
- **Reproduction:** none kept by qti-pie. N1 proved it on the cluster with `Qti`'s own schema, and it was handed to the
  grizzle session as its first priority.
- **Status:** fixed in `f66c05b` (`f66c05b71bb016247163520f29d69de516968c2c`), which qti-pie repins to now (lane T):
  "one reachability over the DDL graph through functions and domains (casts in CHECKs and generated columns, domain
  CHECKs, base chains, constant bodies) grants exactly what a role's writes and reads evaluate; a generated test holds
  granted = reachable against pg_depend on the cluster" (the commit's message). It is not filed by qti-pie, and qti-pie
  took no workaround. The six red database tests need no pantry change (RV-slices §7).

## R87: three writes `grizzle generate` accepts and the write compiler refuses (grizzle `64f0427`)

`grizzle generate`, `grizzle check` and `grizzle migrate` accepted the `Qti` schema. On the migrated schema every import
then failed before any SQL with `ExecutionFailure`, because the write's compile refused it. Each refusal depends on the
schema alone, never on a package's rows, so no import could compile at any rows-first pin: all three refusals are in
grizzle since `941ebfe`, so `4016b69`, `99b6217` and `64f0427` all carry them.

The causes are traced at the vendored commit `64f0427a629ffde66c6d73d5b4ca596fe14001b7`. The files are unchanged at
the grizzle clone's `HEAD` `40963d6`: `git log 64f0427..HEAD -- packages/engine/src/write/frontend/compile.ts
packages/engine/src/write/family/` is empty.

Each reproduction needs nothing of qti-pie and lives in `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/<name>/`.

- `pack/` holds the vendored tarballs, sha256 equal to `vendor/`.
- `case.ts` and `control.ts` hold the two schemas, with one grizzle config per variant.
- `apply.ts` runs `db.apply`'s compile with no database: the adapter's `apply/compile.ts` `writeEntry`, that is
  `compileWrite` under `firstCodebook`.
- `run.sh` prints, for case and control, `grizzle generate` and `grizzle check` of the schema alone, then `node
  apply.ts`, then `grizzle generate` with the same write declared in the login's workload. `run.out` keeps that
  output verbatim, with each exit code.

Status of all three: reported to the grizzle session on 2026-11-06 with these reproductions, and fixed in `f66c05b`
(`f66c05b71bb016247163520f29d69de516968c2c`), which qti-pie repins to now (lane T). That commit carries grizzle's lane
W-fill and its nesting fix: "the fill order is analyzed once in the theory (analysis/theory/fill.ts), refusing an
unfillable copy and an unwritable relation at compile; a tree owning an ordered family and copies of a node's end along
owner axes are writable; a unique over a generated field is refused at compile" (its message). So 1 and 2 are writable,
and 3 is refused when the schema compiles, never first at a write. None is filed as a GitHub issue by qti-pie, and the
reproductions were not re-run at `f66c05b` here. At qti-pie HEAD `ace0abd1`, on grizzle `4b155e8`, the import's full
write program compiles, 152 inserts and none refused (lane R87), so `Node`'s nesting refusal is gone.

### 1. An owner-axis copy of a column the owner fills after its members (grizzle defect)

- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/leaving-copy/`.
- **Case:** a tree `T`, the family `counted(on(M, "owner"), fin(T, "members"))`, and the containment
  `contained(on(M, "owner", "ownerEnd"), on(T, "id", "end"))`, where `ownerEnd: accumulated(natural, 0n)`.
  - `grizzle generate` exits 0, and `grizzle check` prints `Repro: ok`.
  - `ChangeSet.empty(W).insert(T, []).insert(M, [])` is refused with `UnsupportedOperation at ChangeSet.insert:
    M.ownerEnd copies T's end along the owner axis of T.members, a column its owner fills only after its members`.
  - `grizzle generate` with that write in a workload prints `grizzle: refused at stage generate (schema Repro): a
    login's workload does not compile: …` and exits 1.
- **Control:** the same schema copying `start` compiles and generates.
- **Cause:** `filler` in `packages/engine/src/write/family/fold.ts:1059-1072` refuses an `owner` or `ownerCopy` rule
  whose source is a leaving column.
  - A tree's `end` is leaving, as is any column that reads one (`:618-650`). The header states the rule at `:26-31`.
  - The filler is built over every family of the law model, so it refuses whichever nested insert of the schema
    builds it first.
  - It is built only when a ChangeSet's nested insert builds its nesting: `nesting.ts:186`, reached from
    `write/frontend/compile.ts:791-801`. The schema's own compile never builds it.
- **Docs:** README's "How a field is written" (`README.md:1350-1367`), "Families" (`:1507-1561`) and "Limits"
  (`:2458`) never say that an owner-axis copy may not read the owner's `end`, a fold's end or a last-axis copy.
- **Classification:** a grizzle defect. The refusal is a property of the schema alone, yet the schema compiles,
  generates and migrates, and the README does not state it.
- **qti-pie:** `BranchResolution.instanceEnd` copied `Instance.end`. No law read the copy, so it is gone
  (`src/qti/tests.ts`, `1f01419d`). No workaround remains.

### 2. A tree that owns an ordered family (grizzle defect)

- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/tree-ordered-nesting/`.
- **Case:** a tree `T`, and the family `ordered(on(A, "node", "at"), fin(T, "attrs"))`.
  - generate and check pass.
  - `ChangeSet.empty(W).insert(T, [])` is refused with `UnsupportedOperation at ChangeSet.insert: a nested write
    reaches the family T.attrs twice, at depths 1 and 2: families that nest each other have no order to write their
    rows in`.
  - generate with that write in a workload is refused the same way.
- **Controls:** the tree alone compiles and generates, and so does the same ordered family on an owner that is no
  tree.
- **Cause:** the walk in `nestingOf`, `packages/engine/src/write/family/nesting.ts:139-172`.
  - For the tree's own family, the walk pushes the tree's relation again at `depth + 1` (`:167-171`).
  - At that depth it meets the relation's other sequences a second time. Only the tree family itself is skipped
    (`:146`), so every other sequence the tree's relation owns is refused at `:151`.
- **Docs:** README "Families" nests the members of an ordered family or a tree "at any depth", and nothing forbids a
  tree's relation from owning an ordered family.
- **Classification:** a grizzle defect.
- **qti-pie:** `Node` (`nodeTree`) owns `Node.attributes` and `Node.namespaces`, both ordered, so every import is
  refused at `insert Node`.
  - No workaround is taken. Making either family counted would change the model, which is the operator's call.
  - grizzle's nesting fix closes it, in `f66c05b`.

### 3. A key over a field PostgreSQL generates (a grizzle defect at `64f0427`, a limit since `f66c05b`)

- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/generated-unique-key/`.
- **Case:** `relation.generated("R", { id, text }, ["id"], (r) => ({ value: generated(pg.integer, Compute.cast(r.text,
  pg.integer)) }))`, with `key(on(R, "value"))`.
  - generate and check pass.
  - `ChangeSet.empty(W).insert(R, [])` is refused with `an addition to R whose unique objects read caller-written
    fields alone; one over (value) reads value, which PostgreSQL generates and no fact carries`.
- **Control:** `value` written by the caller and proved by `check(…, r.eq(r.value, Compute.cast(r.text,
  pg.integer)))`, under the same key, compiles and generates.
- **Cause:** `refuseGeneratedUnique` in `packages/engine/src/write/frontend/compile.ts:508-528`, called for every
  addition at `:1291-1293`.
- **Docs:** the README says only that a primary key holds no generated field (`README.md:1367`). It says nothing of
  another key over one.
- **Classification:** at `64f0427`, a grizzle defect of the same kind as 1, a schema-only refusal first reached at a
  write. Since `f66c05b`, grizzle refuses a key over a generated field at schema compile by its own design, with a
  typed refusal, so it is a documented limit, not a defect. If the operator or grizzle later calls the refusal itself
  a defect, it becomes one again.
- **qti-pie:** `key(on(MapKeyFloat, "mapping", "value"))` was over the generated cast.
  - **The shape grizzle requires** since `f66c05b`'s compile-time refusal of a key over a generated field: a
    host-written value proved by an E1 check. The host writes `value` (`floatValueOf` in
    `src/import/overlays/mapping.ts`), and `E1_derived` holds it to the same cast (`src/qti/mapping.ts`, `e255b860`).
    The key therefore judges the same values.
  - `floatValueOf` is held to IEEE 754 roundTiesToEven of the key's exact value, computed in BigInt arithmetic, over
    2,000 keys (`src/import/overlays/mapping.test.ts`).

### The guard qti-pie keeps

`grizzle generate` and every `DbLayer` startup compile each program of a login's workload with the same front end as
`db.apply`. Declaring the import's write program in `AuthorApp`'s workload therefore makes a schema whose writes
cannot compile fail at generate, check and startup, never at the first import. That program is `changeSetOf` with no
rows, `importWork` in `src/qti/logins.ts`.

- The patch is `work/rv/r87/workload-guard.patch`. It holds `logins.ts`, the `schema.test.ts` assertion, and three new
  test files: `src/qti/logins.test.ts`, `src/test/gen/assembly.compile.test.ts` and `src/test/gen/assembly.db.test.ts`.
- It lands with the repin to `f66c05b`, which fixes 2. Until that repin, generate refuses with 2's text.
- A cost worth telling grizzle: `workload(role, programs)` takes about 10 s for each ChangeSet program over `Qti`, so a
  workload cannot hold one program per generated package. It took 9.2 s for one program and 72.5 s for five, measured
  2026-11-06 at load average 60. `assembly.compile.test.ts` therefore compiles a spread of six. Every package's
  program has one Description, so the six stand for all of them.

### Not grizzle's: the law oracle raises no reading-87 breach

`src/test/oracle.ts` names `Branch target` and `Branch forward` in its rule table, its sources, its order and its
messages, but nothing raises either one.

- So `oracleVerdict` accepts every package reading 87 refuses. The generator's `p1[s1(i1)] p1=>p1` is one such
