  package: the import writes its resolution, and `Branch_forward` refuses that resolution.
- 28 more of the table's 133 laws have no producer: no `breach("<law>"` and no `law: "<law>"` in the file.
  `Structure identifier`, `Test name key`, `Section parts`, `Selection required`, `Item ref target` and `Variable
  mapping target` are among them.
- `src/test/gen/assembly.test.ts` judges reading 87 by its own model instead, held against the import's rows under the
  laws restated on the host.

## N3: `workload()` and `login()` re-encode whole descriptions (fixed in `f66c05b`)

- **Found by:** our CPU profile of `grizzle check` over `Qti` at `64f0427`
  (`~/.cache/gr8des/integration/prof/CPU.20261106.133120.71117.0.001.cpuprofile`, summarised by `analyze.py` beside
  it). Of its 320.0 s, these are inclusive times: `descText` 31.0 s, `levelOf` 26.5 s and `levelSchema` 26.6 s,
  `workload` 14.7 s, and `login`'s `admitLogin` 4.8 s, of which `sameDesc` is 4.4 s. Evaluating `src/qti/logins.ts`,
  the module that calls `workload` and `login`, takes 19.5 s. An earlier profile of importing `src/import/package.ts`
  (`~/.cache/gr8des/rvp/prof/CPU.20261106.125400.50691.0.001.cpuprofile`, at load average 42) puts 57 s of its 139 s
  in `logins.ts`: 32.7 s in `workload` → `levelSchema` → `descText` and 24.4 s in `login` → `admitLogin` →
  `sameDesc`. That is most of why `src/import/package.ts` takes about 8.6 s to import, against 4.8 s on `main`, as
  Aiden's review of PR #46 measured. R87's workload guard meets the same cost: there, `workload(role, programs)` takes
  about 10 s for each ChangeSet program over `Qti` (the R87 section above).
- **Cause**, at `64f0427` and unchanged at grizzle `main` `40963d6` (`origin/main` on 2026-11-06;
  `git log 64f0427..40963d6` over the five files below is empty):
  - `workload(R, programs)` checks each program's schema with `levelSchema`
    (`packages/engine/src/program/workload/value.ts:133`). For a program over a role, checked against that role,
    `levelOf` compares the two role descriptions by the `descText` of each (`packages/engine/src/program/schema.ts:211`
    to `215`), with no identity test. So each program encodes the role's whole description twice. A role's
    description holds its base schema (`packages/engine/src/ir/desc.ts:935`), and that includes every closed
    relation's rows (`:903`). A program over a schema goes through `isLevelOf` instead
    (`packages/engine/src/schema/levels.ts:45` to `66`), which tests identity first (`:49` to `53`).
  - `login(base, name, roles)` admits each role with `sameDesc(desc.base, baseDesc)`
    (`packages/engine/src/schema/world.ts:554`). `sameDesc` is `orThrow(descText(a)) === orThrow(descText(b))` (`:168`),
    so each role costs two encodes of the whole schema, even when the two arguments are the same object. Role
    admission calls it too (`:264` and `:265`).
  - `descText` is `encode(desc, identityOptions)` (`packages/engine/src/ir/desc.ts:2116` and `2117`), with no memo, so
    every call walks the whole description again.
- **Reproduction** (Node 26, the `64f0427` tarballs and nothing of qti-pie): `n` relations, a role holding them whole,
  a workload of `k` queries over the role and a login. The control is the same `k` queries in a workload over the
  schema itself.

  ```ts
  import { login, pg, query, relation, role, schema, uuid, v, workload } from "@bjornpagen/grizzle";

  const [n, k] = process.argv.slice(2).map(Number);
  const relations = Object.fromEntries(
    Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => [
      `R${String(i)}`,
      relation(
        `R${String(i)}`,
        { a: pg.integer, b: pg.bigint, c: pg.integer, d: pg.boolean, id: uuid },
        ["id"],
      ),
    ]),
  );
  const S = schema("Repro", relations, []);
  const R = role(S, "reader", relations);
  const R0 = relations["R0"];
  const over = (base) =>
    Object.fromEntries(
      Array.from({ length: k }, (_, i) => [
        `q${String(i)}`,
        query(base).rule((r) => {
          const { a, id } = v(R0, "row");
          return r
            .match(R0, { a, id })
            .where(r.eq(id, r.param("id", uuid)))
            .find({ a, id });
        }),
      ]),
    );
  const time = (label, f) => {
    const t0 = performance.now();
    const out = f();
    console.log(`${label}: ${(performance.now() - t0).toFixed(0)} ms`);
    return out;
  };
  const roleQueries = over(R);
  const schemaQueries = over(S);
  const w = time(`n=${n} k=${k} workload(role)`, () => workload(R, roleQueries));
  time(`n=${n} k=${k} workload(schema), control`, () => workload(S, schemaQueries));
  time(`n=${n} k=${k} login(schema, [role])`, () => login(S, "app", [R], { workload: [w] }));
  ```

  `node repro.ts <n> <k>` for `n` of 50 and 150 and `k` of 1, 10 and 40, at load average 80. The output is verbatim:

  ```text
  n=50 k=1 workload(role): 97 ms
  n=50 k=1 workload(schema), control: 1 ms
  n=50 k=1 login(schema, [role]): 135 ms
  n=50 k=10 workload(role): 485 ms
  n=50 k=10 workload(schema), control: 2 ms
  n=50 k=10 login(schema, [role]): 263 ms
  n=50 k=40 workload(role): 1088 ms
  n=50 k=40 workload(schema), control: 2 ms
  n=50 k=40 login(schema, [role]): 22 ms
  n=150 k=1 workload(role): 77 ms
  n=150 k=1 workload(schema), control: 0 ms
  n=150 k=1 login(schema, [role]): 231 ms
  n=150 k=10 workload(role): 567 ms
  n=150 k=10 workload(schema), control: 2 ms
  n=150 k=10 login(schema, [role]): 351 ms
  n=150 k=40 workload(role): 2561 ms
  n=150 k=40 workload(schema), control: 1 ms
  n=150 k=40 login(schema, [role]): 337 ms
  ```

  A workload over the role grows with the number of programs times the schema's size. The control stays near 0 ms,
  because `isLevelOf`'s identity test answers before any encode. `Qti`'s 65 closed relations make each encode far
  larger than these plain relations do. That is why the profiles above spend tens of seconds where this
  reproduction spends milliseconds.

- **Classification:** a grizzle performance defect. Neither the README nor the agent guide states a cost for
  `workload` or `login`. Row 64 is the same family: role descriptions re-encoded on every `ChangeSet.insert`,
  recorded as fixed upstream on 2026-11-05.
- **Status:** fixed in `f66c05b` (`f66c05b71bb016247163520f29d69de516968c2c`), which qti-pie repins to now (lane T). It
  was reported to the grizzle session on 2026-11-06 with the profile, and grizzle's lane PERF fixed it: "each schema
  carries a content digest taken at build, so login, role, workload and every level check compare digests; DbLayer.make
  compiles one installation for all roles" (the commit's message). On PERF's measurement at 150 relations,
  `DbLayer.make` falls from 43.7 s to 8.0 s, and `grizzle migrate`'s time before it connects falls from 70.3 s to
  0.93 s. The reproduction above was not re-run at `f66c05b` here. It is not filed by qti-pie, and qti-pie took no
  workaround: building `AuthorApp` lazily would only have moved the cost to the first database use.

## `GoverningOf` has no dense coordinate its readers hold (a documented limit, lane CGD)

- **What.** `GoverningOf` is (version, kind, governs) → governor, keyed over 2 · |NodeKind| · |TypeName| = 2 · 644 · 444
  = 571,872 cells. It holds 4,516 rows (0.8% dense), and its 11-bit cells come to 786,332 B, 1.57 MB as hex.
- **Why it doesn't densify.** grizzle's remedy for a sparse closed relation is a dense coordinate such as a slot, whose
  codes run 0 to n − 1 per owner (README "Limits", `README.md:2562` to `2565` at `f66c05b`: "choose coordinates that
  make it dense, such as slots"). That needs the reader to hold the dense coordinate. `Follow`'s readers hold a
  position, and AttributeGrammar's (after DK) a slot from `AttributeSlotOf`. Every reader of `GoverningOf` holds (kind,
  governs), with `governs` a `TypeName` code, and a kind's lawful types are a sparse, kind-specific subset of TypeName:
  - 1,256 of the 1,288 (version, kind) blocks of declared kinds have one row;
  - 26 have 9 to 42;
  - the three open element classes have all 444.

  So no permutation of TypeName makes every kind's types a prefix. The index from (kind, type) to a slot would be the
  same sparse product again.

- **What would densify it.** `Node` would carry the type's slot among its kind's `XsiType` rows, `typeSlot`, written by
  the host, and `Attribute` would copy it as it copies `governs`. `GoverningOf` would then be (version, kind, typeSlot)
  → (governs, governor), 4,516 cells × 20 bits, about 11 KB. Its `governs` payload would also state N12's containment
  of (version, kind, governs) in `XsiType`, which would make `XsiType` (71,492 B) unnecessary. That is a change to
  `Node`, `Attribute`, the write path, the folds and every reader of `governs`, beyond DK's scope, so DK leaves
  `GoverningOf` keyed as it is.
- **Classification.** A documented limit, met by the model's coordinates: grizzle behaves as its README states, so
  nothing is filed.
- **Status.** A post-tier follow-up, by the coordinator's ruling of 2026-11-06: "GoverningOf densify: not now". It is
  a schema change to `Node`, the write path and the folds, made right before the first database tier on the new pin,
  so it waits until that tier is green and is then scheduled.

## `@grizzle/testing`'s `matching` builds every class before it refuses an unbounded repetition (performance, lane JAN)

- **What:** `matching(/^(?:[\t\n\r -\uD7FF\uE000-\uFFFD\u{10000}-\u{10FFFF}]*)$/u)` throws `PatternRefused: … repeats
  without a bound: write {min,max}`, but only after 2.8 to 4.3 s, at a load average near 45 on the shared laptop. The
  refusal depends on the pattern's syntax alone (`*`, `+`, `{n,}`), yet it lands after the generator has expanded XML's
  `Char` class, more than a million code points with the astral plane. A bounded pattern over the same class,
  `String256DType`'s `{0,255}`, took 6.8 s before it drew.
- **Measured:** in `src/test/lexical.oracle.test.ts` (a scratch probe, since deleted): xs:anySimpleType 3590 ms,
  xs:normalizedString 4332 ms, xs:normalizedString (reading 132) 4223 ms, URLDType (reading 124) 2762 ms,
  alphabet.datatype 3326 ms, every one refused; String256DType 6828 ms in `matching`, 1 ms in `sample`.
- **qti-pie's workaround (8691dd27):** the test skips a pattern that repeats without a bound outside its classes and
  escapes before calling `matching`. The probes come out byte-identical.
- **Classification:** not triaged against grizzle's source; a performance note, not a correctness defect. A refusal
  that needs no class could be checked before the classes are built.

## LP: AuthorAppLayer's startup, split (grizzle `4b155e8`, rechecked at `27c4cd2`)

Lane LP measured `AuthorAppLayer`'s build on scratch copies of qti-pie `ddeb5147` and `984453d7`, with no database:
the port is 1, and the pool opens no session while the layer is built. Each figure is process CPU seconds. Wall time
ran 3.5 to 6 times CPU at load 42 to 73.

| Stage                                                                               | CPU s        |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------ |
| `logins.ts` import, which is the Qti schema module (before `ef7423f0`)              | 10.5 to 13.5 |
| `artifactsUnder`, the base artifacts                                                | 24.1         |
| `loginAccess(importWork)`: the write front end, to compare access with the snapshot | 7.8          |
| `compileRoleWorkload(importWork)`: the whole write, one variant                     | 8.0          |
| every `db.apply` compile of the import ChangeSet                                    | 7.9          |
| the same compile of a ChangeSet of one insert                                       | 8.2          |
| whole layer build with `importWork`                                                 | 48.5         |
| whole layer build without it (dropped in scratch, snapshot regenerated)             | 30.1         |

### LP-1. The write front end decides every relation's unique objects on every compile (grizzle cost, fixed in `dec8ab3`)

- **Cause:** 96% of a write compile is one line.
  - `compile`'s `writable` map (`packages/engine/src/write/frontend/compile.ts:730-732`) calls `relationUniques`
    (`write/insert/unique.ts:220-222`) for every ordinary relation of the base, written or not.
  - That calls `beyondPrimary` (`laws/model/uniques.ts:320-325`).
  - It compares unique objects pairwise through `subsumes` (`uniques.ts:303-312`).
  - `subsumes` calls `canonicalEquals` (`core/canonical.ts:692-699`), which encodes both guard predicates on every
    comparison.
- **Where the time goes in Qti:** one relation, `Attribute`, takes 8.25 CPU s. It has 57 unique objects beyond its
  primary key, carrying 97 guards. The other 161 relations together take under 0.03 s.
- **Every other phase is small.** In a profile of the import's write compile:
  - strata scheduling: 0.1%;
  - member classes: 0.5%;
  - families (`familyProgram`): 0.4%;
  - lowering: `optimizedArtifact` 1.7%, conservative realization and artifact 0.7%, `writeFacts` 0.1%.
- **The same work repeats around startup:**
  - The law model already runs it for every relation in `#refuseGeneratedKeys` (`laws/model/model.ts:898-917`, at
    `:902`).
  - `compileLaws` runs `specialize` twice (`model.ts:1552-1565`, at `:1555` and `:1561`).
  - So a layer build pays it four times: twice in `compileLaws`, once in `loginAccess`
    (`program/workload/compile.ts:187`) and once in `compileRoleWorkload` (`adapter/db/workload.ts:136-137`).
  - Every `db.apply` pays it again, because "grizzle keeps no cache of programs" (`adapter/apply/compile.ts:26-27`;
    `apply/index.ts:134`). qti never runs the layer's compiled form.
- **Check:** the same subsumption with each predicate encoded once takes 0.10 CPU s, against 7.90, and it matches
  `beyondPrimary` object for object on all 162 relations. It measures the redundancy only. The ruling is to delete
  work, not to cache it.
- **Work that can be deleted:**
  - Record each relation's objects beyond its primary key in `RelationLaws` when the law model decides them
    (`model.ts:902`), and read them in `relationUniques`.
  - Drop the provisional `specialize`'s `#refuseGeneratedKeys` and `relationLaws` pass (`model.ts:1555`).
  - Compare guards without re-encoding them pair by pair.
- **Reproduction:** `~/.cache/gr8des/integration/grizzle-repros/layer-workload/` (`run.sh`, with output in `run.out`).
  - It builds a schema of r relations, each with k `key(on(select(Ti, φj), "a"))` guarded keys, and a lawless `U`.
    Guards may read a closed relation of w rows.
  - The login's workload is one of: none, a ChangeSet of one insert into `U`, or one into `U` and every Ti.
  - `DbLayer.make` is built with no database.
  - At r=4, k=32 under the one-insert workload: `compileLaws` is 83% `beyondPrimary`, `loginAccess` 96-99%, and
    `compileRoleWorkload` 94-99%, though the ChangeSet writes only `U`.
  - The hot files are byte-identical from `4b155e8` to `27c4cd2`, and `run.out` holds both pins.
- **Fixed in grizzle `dec8ab3`** (`dec8ab32289d91d0190c676e6232ca89a74697a2`), lane K's `beyondPrimary` fix: startup
  decides the keys beyond the primary once, 6.06 s down to 0.39 s at 128 guarded keys, under a call-count gate.
  qti-pie repinned to it in `89f1b5e9` (repin D). There `logins.test.ts` alone took 71 s, against 192 s at `27c4cd2`,
  and R87's `compile.ts` 30 s, against 184 to 337 s.

### LP-2. A closed-id literal carries its roster into every canonical encode (grizzle cost, its cutover ruled)

- Attribute's guard predicates encode to 23,000-62,000 characters each, some to 100,000-392,000.
- interactions.ts:1709's encodes to 2,979,763 characters.
- Each comparison of a closed-id field with a literal carries the whole roster, so every canonical encode of such a
  predicate costs as much as the roster. That bears on every canonical encode, not only `beyondPrimary`.
- **grizzle's ruling:** a closed-id literal will name its roster by identity, encoded once with the schema, under a
  gate that an encode's size does not grow with the roster. It is a hard cutover still to land, and fingerprints will
  change.

### LP-3. Four of Attribute's keys are implied by wider ones (qti, after the database tier)

Each selection was evaluated exactly over the grammar's 78,424 attribute rows: (version, kind, governs, name, slot),
from each GoverningOf row's model's slots, with the closed reads evaluated from their rows. Script and output:
`~/.cache/gr8des/integration/lp-attribute-audit/attraudit.ts` and `attraudit-exact.out`, beside `attr.ts`, `attr.json`
and `attr.out`, the dump of Attribute's unique objects it reads (it runs from a pantry package directory). Four keys lie
inside a wider key over the same field, so each is one key stated twice:

| Narrower key                                                                    | Inside                                                                        | Its containment                                                              |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| metadata.ts:935 `key(on(objectIdentifiers, "node"))`, item and test `xml:lang`  | identity.ts:1015 `key(on(langAttributes, "node"))`, `xml:lang` everywhere     | copy-back beside it; grizzle's `beyondPrimary` already drops 935 as subsumed |
| package.ts:600 `key(on(manifestIdentifiers, "node"))`, `cp:manifest@identifier` | identity.ts:929 `key(on(idAttributes, "node"))`, one ID attribute per element | copy-back at :601 into `ManifestId`                                          |
| package.ts:610 `key(on(resourceIdentifiers, "node"))`, `cp:resource@identifier` | identity.ts:929                                                               | copy-back at :611 into `Resource`                                            |
| html.ts:369 `key(on(headerIds, "node"))`, `th@id`                               | identity.ts:929                                                               | `contained(on(TableHeader, "node"), on(headerIds, "node"))` at :371          |

- **Ruling (the Mind, 2026-11-06):** they go by the one-way rule. Removing them restates their containments, which is
  a schema change, so it lands after the database tier is green, like GoverningOf.
- **Plan, for each one:**
  1. Retarget its copy-back or containment onto the wider selection.
  2. Prove the old and new laws equal by exhaustion over the 78,424 rows, as `ace0abd1` did for the copy-backs.
  3. Delete the narrower key.
- **Open question:** grizzle requires a containment's target to hold "a key over the same selection among its terms"
  (README, Rules). That rule may be what forced these keys. If grizzle accepts the wider key for the narrower target,
  that settles it. If it refuses, it is a grizzle question, which the Mind will ask.
- declarations.ts:536 `key(on(identifiers, "node"))` and identity.ts:929 overlap on `qti-context-declaration` and
  `qti-response-declaration`'s `identifier`, but neither lies inside the other. They are two keys, and both stay.
- Every other pair of Attribute's 52 keys over one field list is disjoint.

### LP-4. The Qti import's repeated domain compiles (qti, fixed in `ef7423f0`)

- **Split of the import before the fix** (10.5 CPU s, profile at `ddeb5147`):

  | CPU s | What                                 | Owner   |
  | ----- | ------------------------------------ | ------- |
  | 3.86  | grammar/domains.ts `compileUncached` | qti     |
  | 1.26  | schema builders                      | grizzle |
  | 1.18  | closed relation admission            | grizzle |
  | 0.91  | grammar/content.ts `installContent`  | qti     |
  | 0.90  | grammar/install.ts `handleIn`        | qti     |
  | 0.78  | GC                                   |         |
  | 0.54  | other grammar                        | qti     |
  | 0.41  | other                                | grizzle |
  | 0.36  | module load                          | node    |

- **Cause:**
  - `compileUncached` ran 1692 times for 252 distinct definitions. Spec readings built a new definition per (version,
    declaration), and `compile`'s cache was keyed by object identity. The language tags of reading 129 alone
    recompiled 551, 368 and 337 times.
  - `handleIn` searched a roster with `find` for every generated row.
- **Fix:**
  - `domainReader` compiles each distinct read definition once, in one pass, and `compile`'s memo is deleted.
  - `handleLookup` builds one Map per roster.
- **Result:**
  - Qti's import fell from 12.3-13.5 to 5.4-6.6 CPU s.
  - Its fingerprint is unchanged: `f5cbad00e2e3f5f51b42387a6bac9e28992e37c8ab7d910fff4bdd2686400d85`.
- **Guard:** `src/grammar/attributes.work.test.ts`, with planted bugs shown failing.
- **LP's last commit,** `d13a8389`, finds the sentinel-range characters a document writes with one regex in crust, not
  a walk over every code point.
